, restate=[name=Simulation]CalcMELLSimulatesCalcParigotViaTTranslation)
.

If
M\to_{\mu}N
, then
{M}^{\mathsf{T}}_{k}\twoheadrightarrow_{\mathsf{\lambda_{\textsf{{MELL}}}}}{N}^{\mathsf{T}}_{k}
.

Q-Translation.

The Q-translation, introduced also in
[
14
]
,
is an alternative embedding of classical logic into linear logic.
While the T-translation is a
call-by-name
translation,
it is well-known that the Q-translation is instead a
call-by-value
translation.
To study the Q-translation, we need to first recall a notion of call-by-value
reduction for the
\lambda\mu
-calculus
[
26
]
.

The types and terms of
\lambda\mu_{V}
are the same as those of
\lambda\mu
.
We use the notations
M{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\bm{\{}}a{\lhd}V{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\bm{\}}}
for the usual
(right-)structural substitution,
M{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\bm{\{}}a{\lhd^{\star}}V{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\bm{\}}}
for
left-structural substitution.
Reduction in
\lambda\mu_{V}
, denoted
\to_{\mu_{V}}
, is defined as the contextual closure of the following reduction axioms:

\begin{array}[]{llll}(\lambda x.\,M)\,\,V&\mapsto_{\beta_{V}}&M{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}x:=V{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}\\
(\mu{a}.M)\,\,V&\mapsto_{\mu_{V}}&\mu{a}.(M{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\bm{\{}}a{\lhd}V{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\bm{\}}})\\
V\,\,(\mu{a}.M)&\mapsto_{\mu_{V}^{\prime}}&\mu{a}.(M{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\bm{\{}}a{\lhd^{\star}}V{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\bm{\}}})\\
\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{llll}[a](\mu{b}.M)&\mapsto_{\rho}&M{\color[rgb]{0.91,0.41,0.17}\bm{\{}}b{\curvearrowleft}a{\color[rgb]{0.91,0.41,0.17}\bm{\}}}\\
\mu{a}.[a]M&\mapsto_{\theta}&M&(a\notin\mathsf{fv}(M))\end{array}

The Q-translation of types is defined as follows:

\begin{array}[]{r@{\hspace{.1cm}}c@{\hspace{.1cm}}l@{\hspace{.5cm}}r@{\hspace{.1cm}}c@{\hspace{.1cm}}l@{\hspace{.5cm}}r@{\hspace{.1cm}}c@{\hspace{.1cm}}l}{{\perp}}^{\mathsf{Q}}\hskip 2.84544pt&:=\hfil\hskip 2.84544pt&{!}{{\perp}}\hfil\hskip 14.22636pt&{\alpha}^{\mathsf{Q}}\hskip 2.84544pt&:=\hfil\hskip 2.84544pt&{!}{\alpha}\hfil\hskip 14.22636pt&{(A\supset B)}^{\mathsf{Q}}\hskip 2.84544pt&:=\hfil\hskip 2.84544pt&{!}{(({A}^{\mathsf{Q}})^{\perp}\invamp{?}{B}^{\mathsf{Q}})}\\
\end{array}

We define
{A}^{\mathsf{\underline{Q}}}
as the function such that
{A}^{\mathsf{Q}}={!}{{A}^{\mathsf{\underline{Q}}}}
. Values are defined as:

V::=x\,|\,\lambda x.\,M
.
The
{(\cdot)}^{\mathsf{Q}}
-translation for values and terms, are given mutually recursively by

\begin{array}[]{rcl}{x}^{\mathsf{\underline{Q}}}&:=&x\\
{(\lambda x.\,M)}^{\mathsf{\underline{Q}}}&:=&{\invamp}(a,b).{M}^{\mathsf{Q}}_{k}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}{!}{x}:=a{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}{!}{k}:=b{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{rcl}{V}^{\mathsf{Q}}_{k}&:=&{!}{{V}^{\mathsf{\underline{Q}}}}\lightning k\\
{(M\,N)}^{\mathsf{Q}}_{k}&:=&{M}^{\mathsf{Q}}_{k^{\prime\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}k^{\prime\prime}:={?}v.{N}^{\mathsf{Q}}_{k^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}k^{\prime}:=v{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}{!}{k}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}\\
{([a]M)}^{\mathsf{Q}}_{k}&:=&{M}^{\mathsf{Q}}_{k^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}k^{\prime}:=a{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}\lightning k\\
{(\mu{a}.M)}^{\mathsf{Q}}_{k}&:=&{M}^{\mathsf{Q}}_{k^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}k^{\prime}:=\star{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}a:=k{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}\par\end{array}

Lemma 22

(name=Type preservation
[Proof on pg.
F.1.2
]
, restate=[name=Type preservation]TypePreservationForQTranslation)
.

If
\Gamma\vdash M:A\mid\Sigma
holds in
\lambda\mu_{V}
, then
{\Gamma}^{\mathsf{\underline{Q}}},({\Sigma}^{\mathsf{Q}})^{\perp},k:({A}^{\mathsf{Q}})^{\perp};\cdot\vdash{M}^{\mathsf{Q}}:{\perp}
holds in
\lambda_{\textsf{{MELL}}}
.

Proposition 23

(name=Simulation
[Proof on pg.
F.1.2
]
, restate=[name=Simulation]CalcMELLSimulatesCalcParigotViaQTranslation)
.

If
M\to_{\mu_{V}}N
, then
{M}^{\mathsf{Q}}_{k}\doteq_{\mathsf{\lambda_{\textsf{{MELL}}}}}{N}^{\mathsf{Q}}_{k}
, where
\doteq_{\mathsf{\lambda_{\textsf{{MELL}}}}}
denotes the reflexive, symmetric, and transitive closure of
\to_{\mathsf{\lambda_{\textsf{{MELL}}}}}
.

5.2
Simulation of Curien and Herbelin’s
\overline{\lambda}\mu\tilde{\mu}
via the T-translation

Curien and Herbelin
[
13
]
introduced a term assignment for classical logic in sequent calculus style that has since become a cornerstone in the study of computational interpretations of classical logic. Its merit is that it reveals hidden dualities in the programming language from the natural symmetries of the sequent calculus (
cf.

[
16
]
for a tutorial).

We start by recalling the sets of types and terms of
\bar{\lambda}\mu\tilde{\mu}
,
which are are defined as follows.
Note that terms are split into three syntactic categories (terms, co–terms and commands):

\begin{array}[]{lrcl}\text{(Types)}&A,B&::=&{\alpha}\mid{A\supset B}\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{lrcl}\text{(Terms)}&v,v^{\prime}&::=&x\mid\mu{\alpha}.\,c\mid\lambda x.\,v\\
\text{(Co-terms)}&e,e^{\prime}&::=&\alpha\mid v\cdot e\\
\text{(Commands)}&c,c^{\prime}&::=&\langle v\mid e\rangle\end{array}

The typing system consists of three judgements
\Gamma\vdash v:A\mid\Delta
,
\Gamma\mid e:A\vdash\Delta
,
and
c:(\Gamma\vdash\Delta)
,
with the following typing rules:

\begin{array}[]{c}\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 40.22893pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=80.45786pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{VarR}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma,x:A\vdash x:A\mid\Delta$}}}}\end{array}\quad\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 40.91068pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=81.82135pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{VarL}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma\mid\alpha:A\vdash\alpha:A,\Delta$}}}}\end{array}\quad\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma\vdash v:A\mid\Delta\quad\Gamma\mid e:B\vdash\Delta$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=117.94847pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{AppL}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 11.45827pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma\mid(v\cdot e):A\supset B\vdash\Delta$}}}}\end{array}\\
\\
\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty c:(\Gamma\vdash\alpha:B,\Delta)$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=73.94992pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{ActivR}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 2.82056pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash\mu{\alpha}.\,c:B,\Delta$}}}}\end{array}\quad\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 5.41669pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma,x:A\vdash v:B\mid\Delta$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=91.36877pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{Abs}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash\lambda x.\,v:A\supset B\mid\Delta$}}}}\end{array}\quad\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma\vdash v:A\mid\Delta\quad\Gamma\mid e:A\vdash\Delta$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=117.36168pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{cut}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 26.73592pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\langle v\mid e\rangle:(\Gamma\vdash\Delta)$}}}}\end{array}\par\end{array}

Reduction in
\bar{\lambda}\mu\tilde{\mu}
is given by the contextual closure of
the following two reduction axioms, where
v_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}x:=v_{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}
and
c{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}\alpha:=e{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}
are standard substitution:

\begin{array}[]{c@{\hspace{1cm}}c}\begin{array}[]{llll}\langle\lambda x.\,v_{1}\mid v_{2}\cdot e\rangle&\mapsto&\langle v_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}x:=v_{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}\mid e\rangle\end{array}\hfil\hskip 28.45274pt&\begin{array}[]{llll}\langle\mu{\alpha}.\,c\mid e\rangle&\mapsto&c{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}\alpha:=e{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}\end{array}\end{array}

The T-translation of Danos
et al.

[
14
]

is defined for formulae, judgements and terms of
\bar{\lambda}\mu\tilde{\mu}
,
as follows:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}{\alpha}^{\mathsf{T}}&:=&\alpha\\
{(A\supset B)}^{\mathsf{T}}&:=&{?}{!}{({A}^{\mathsf{T}})^{\perp}}\invamp{?}{B}^{\mathsf{T}}\\
\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{rcl}c:(\Gamma\vdash\Delta)&\mapsto&{?}{\Gamma}^{\mathsf{T}},{{\Delta}^{\mathsf{T}}}^{\bot};\cdot\vdash{c}^{\mathsf{T}}:\bot\\
\Gamma\mid e:A\vdash\Delta&\mapsto&{?}{\Gamma}^{\mathsf{T}},{{\Delta}^{\mathsf{T}}}^{\bot};\cdot\vdash{e}^{\mathsf{T}}:{!}{({{A}^{\mathsf{T}}}^{\perp})}\\
\Gamma\vdash v:A\mid\Delta&\mapsto&{?}{\Gamma}^{\mathsf{T}},{{\Delta}^{\mathsf{T}}}^{\bot};\cdot\vdash{v}^{\mathsf{T}}:{?}{A}^{\mathsf{T}}\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{rcl}{x}^{\mathsf{T}}&:=&x\\
{(\mu{\alpha}.\,c)}^{\mathsf{T}}&:=&{?}\alpha.{c}^{\mathsf{T}}\\
{(\lambda x.\,v)}^{\mathsf{T}}&:=&{?}u.(u\lightning{\invamp}(a,b).({v}^{\mathsf{T}}\lightning b{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}{!}{x}:=a{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}))\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\\
{\alpha}^{\mathsf{T}}&:=&{!}{\alpha}\\
{(v\cdot e)}^{\mathsf{T}}&:=&{!}{\langle{!}{{v}^{\mathsf{T}}},{e}^{\mathsf{T}}\rangle}\\
{\langle v\mid e\rangle}^{\mathsf{T}}&:=&{v}^{\mathsf{T}}\lightning{e}^{\mathsf{T}}\end{array}

Lemma 24

(name=Type preservation
[Proof on pg.
F.2
]
, restate=[name=Type preservation]TypePreservationForTTranslationForBarLambdaMuTildeMu)
.

1.

If
c:(\Gamma\vdash\Delta)
holds in
\bar{\lambda}\mu\tilde{\mu}
, then

{?}{\Gamma}^{\mathsf{T}},{{\Delta}^{\mathsf{T}}}^{\bot};\cdot\vdash{c}^{\mathsf{T}}:\bot
holds in
\lambda_{\textsf{{MELL}}}
.

2.

If
\Gamma\mid e:A\vdash\Delta
holds in
\bar{\lambda}\mu\tilde{\mu}
, then

{?}{\Gamma}^{\mathsf{T}},{{\Delta}^{\mathsf{T}}}^{\bot};\cdot\vdash{e}^{\mathsf{T}}:{!}{({{A}^{\mathsf{T}}}^{\perp})}
holds in
\lambda_{\textsf{{MELL}}}
.

3.

If
\Gamma\vdash v:A\mid\Delta
holds in
\bar{\lambda}\mu\tilde{\mu}
, then

{?}{\Gamma}^{\mathsf{T}},{{\Delta}^{\mathsf{T}}}^{\bot};\cdot\vdash{v}^{\mathsf{T}}:{?}{A}^{\mathsf{T}}
holds in
\lambda_{\textsf{{MELL}}}
.

Proposition 25

(name=Simulation
[Proof on pg.
F.2
]
, restate=[name=Simulation]CalcMELLSimulatesCalcParigotViaTTranslationForBarLambdaMuTildeMu)
.

If
t\to_{\bar{\lambda}\mu\tilde{\mu}}s
, then
{t}^{\mathsf{T}}_{k}\twoheadrightarrow_{\mathsf{\lambda_{\textsf{{MELL}}}}}{s}^{\mathsf{T}}_{k}
.

6
Related Work and Conclusions

Proofs-as-Processes.

Although not the focus of our work, there is a large body of work on interpreting cut-elimination in terms of concurrent or parallel programs. This program was perhaps kickstarted by Abramsky
[
1
]
and likely influenced by Girard himself
[
18
]
(having mentioned that “
connectives of linear logic have an obvious meaning in terms of parallel computation…
”). Since then many works have been presented in this area. Relatively recent highlights include the tight correspondence between intuitionistic linear logic and session types by Caires and Pfenning
[
12
]
, its extension to classical linear logic by Wadler
[
28
,
29
]
and modeling asynchronous communication via a term assignment for a multi-conclusion natural deduction presentation of
MLL

[
4
]
. Further references are provided in
[
4
, Sec.11]
.

Proofs-as-Functions.

Troelstra
[
27
]
introduces a one-sided natural deduction presentation of
LL
. However, par and why-not are not included, and normalization of proofs is not studied. Martini and Masini
[
22
]
study the par connective, arguing that it had not received any attention from a natural deduction perspective because of the “
apparent impossibility to formulate a suitable introduction-elimination pair, when sticking to single conclusion systems
”. Our rules for par are similar to theirs (the elimination rules are exactly the same, the introduction rule is not but is provably equivalent). A term assignment is not considered in their work nor are the exponentials.
Ramalho Martins et al
[
23
]
also introduce a natural deduction presentation for full
LL
. All connectives are considered, including the additives. Only weak normalisation is proved and no term calculus or computational interpretation of proof normalisation is discussed.
Albrecht et al
[
2
]
propose a term assignment for
LL
. They include an inference rule called
swap
that corresponds to our (derived) notion of contra-substitution but the similarities with our work stop there. There are several shortcomings with their proposed system
{\cal N}
including a global restriction on well-formedness of derivations (
cf.
Sec. 2.4 on pg 221 of
op.cit.
) that requires a complicated notion of term reduction (parallel reduction) to avoid breaking it
4
4

4

Moreover, we believe
{\cal N}
is incomplete with respect to linear logic. The issue is in the case that shows that linear logic contraction is captured in
{\cal N}
. Case (?C) of Lemma A.1 does not consider the situation where
n=0
in the proof of
\vdash{?}\alpha,{?}\alpha,\beta_{1}\ldots,\beta_{n}
. This case does not seem to be provable in
{\cal N}
.
.
Bierman
[
11
]
presents a Parigot style calculus for full
LL
which relies on a multiple-conclusion natural deduction. The term assignment makes use of Parigot’s structural substitution. It includes many commuting conversions (most of which are not listed). It also mentions the possibility of defining a rule for par but no discussion is present in the paper (
[
11
, Sec.3.4.]
).
Mazurak and Zdancewic
[
24
]
propose a term assignment for
MLL
. They introduce a functional language with concurrency primitives, the latter motivated by a parallel rendering of Felleisen’s control and abort control operators. There are various items that set our work apart from this. We address
MELL
which includes the exponentials. Our system
\lambda_{\textsf{{MELL}}}
is shown to be complete with respect to
MELL
; in
[
24
]
this question is left open for their calculus. Finally, our operational semantics arises purely from proof normalization, whereas their calculus includes various term constructors that do not arise from purely proof theoretical means. Examples are the term constructors for the new binder and parallel composition of processes. Finally, there are works that provide a computational intepretation of
LL
based on the proof-nets presentation. A recent contribution is Kesner’s
[
21
]
where terms are associated to proof-nets. See
op.cit
for further references on the proof-net approach.

Future Work.

Extending our approach to include the additives is not immediate since contra-substitution relies crucially on linear terms for its definition. This requires further investigation. Also, simulating our calculus, in particular structural equivalence, via proof-nets would be of interest. Extending
\lambda_{\textsf{{MELL}}}
with second-order propositional quantifiers should be unproblematic. However, adding fixed points in the style of Baelde et al
[
6
,
5
]
sounds more challenging.

Appendix A
A Contraposition-Based Calculus for
MLL

\CompletenessSoundnessMLL

*

Proof.

Soundness.

By induction on the derivation of the judgment:

1.

m-ax
:
Then
t=a:A
and
\Gamma=(a:{A})
and the derivation is of the form:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 23.63292pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\vphantom{{}^{@}}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=53.90475pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-ax}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle a:A\vdash a:A$}}}}\end{array}

Hence we can take:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 13.72217pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\vphantom{{}^{@}}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=34.08325pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{l-ax}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\vdash{A,A^{\perp}}$}}}}\end{array}

2.

m-i
\otimes
:
Then
t=\langle s,r\rangle
and
A=B\otimes C

and the derivation is of the form:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma\vdash s:B\quad\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash r:C$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=87.95354pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-i$\otimes$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 3.3332pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\langle s,r\rangle:B\otimes C$}}}}\end{array}

Hence:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\vdash{\Gamma^{\perp},B}\quad\vdash{\Gamma^{\prime\perp},C}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=76.14366pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{l-$\otimes$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 1.94449pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\vdash{\Gamma^{\prime\perp},\Gamma^{\perp},B\otimes C}$}}}}\end{array}

3.

m-e
\otimes
:
The derivation is of the form

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash s:B\otimes C\quad\Gamma^{\prime\prime},b:B,c:C\vdash r:A$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=162.91055pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-e$\otimes$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 34.42119pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\vdash{r}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\langle b,c\rangle:=s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}:A$}}}}\end{array}

Hence by IH:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\begin{array}[]{c}\text{IH }\\
\vdash{\Gamma^{\prime\perp},B\otimes C}\end{array}\begin{array}[]{c}\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\begin{array}[]{c}\text{IH }\\
\vdash{\Gamma^{\prime\prime\perp},B^{\perp},C^{\perp},A}\end{array}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=81.00479pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{l-$\multimap$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.37508pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\vdash{\Gamma^{\prime\prime\perp},B\multimap C^{\perp},A}$}}}}\end{array}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=176.25958pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{l-cut}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 60.03261pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\vdash{\Gamma^{\prime\perp},\Gamma^{\prime\prime\perp},A}$}}}}\end{array}

4.

m-i
\multimap
:
Then
t=\lambda b.\,t^{\prime}
and
A=B\multimap C
and the derivation is of the form:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 7.08336pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma,b:B\vdash t^{\prime}:C$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=77.074pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-i$\multimap$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash\lambda b.\,t^{\prime}:B\multimap C$}}}}\end{array}

Therefore by IH:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\begin{array}[]{c}\text{IH }\\
\vdash{\Gamma^{\perp},B^{\perp},C}\end{array}\par$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=58.69925pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{l-$\multimap$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.37508pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\vdash{\Gamma^{\perp},B\multimap C}$}}}}\end{array}

5.

m-e
\multimap_{1}
:
Then
t=s\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,r
and the derivation is of the form:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash s:B\multimap A\quad\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\vdash r:B$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=114.67784pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-e$\multimap_{1}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 22.53119pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\vdash s\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,r:A$}}}}\end{array}

Hence by IH:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\begin{array}[]{c}\text{IH }\\
\vdash{\Gamma^{\prime\perp},B\multimap A}\end{array}\begin{array}[]{c}\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\begin{array}[]{c}\text{IH }\\
\vdash{\Gamma^{\prime\prime\perp},B}\end{array}\begin{array}[]{c}\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 13.72217pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\vphantom{{}^{@}}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=34.08325pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{l-ax}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\vdash{A,A^{\perp}}$}}}}\end{array}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=110.72565pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{l-$\otimes$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 17.63895pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\vdash{\Gamma^{\prime\prime\perp},A,B\otimes A^{\perp}}$}}}}\end{array}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=206.72905pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{l-cut}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 75.26733pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\vdash{\Gamma^{\prime\perp},\Gamma^{\prime\prime\perp},A}$}}}}\end{array}

6.

m-e
\multimap_{2}
:
Then
t={s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}r}
and the derivation is of the form:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash s:A^{\perp}\multimap B\quad\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\vdash r:B^{\perp}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=123.40013pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-e$\multimap_{2}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 29.94778pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\vdash s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}r:{A}$}}}}\end{array}

Therefore by IH:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\begin{array}[]{c}\text{IH }\\
\vdash{\Gamma^{\prime\perp},A^{\perp}\multimap B}\end{array}\begin{array}[]{c}\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\begin{array}[]{c}\text{IH }\\
\vdash{\Gamma^{\prime\prime\perp},B}\end{array}\begin{array}[]{c}\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 13.72217pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\vphantom{{}^{@}}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=34.08325pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{l-ax}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\vdash{A,A^{\perp}}$}}}}\end{array}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=110.72565pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{l-$\otimes$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 15.87503pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\vdash{\Gamma^{\prime\prime\perp},A,A^{\perp}\otimes B^{\perp}}$}}}}\end{array}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=209.70134pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{l-cut}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 76.75348pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\vdash{\Gamma^{\prime\perp},\Gamma^{\prime\prime\perp},A}$}}}}\end{array}

Completeness.

By hypothesis,
\vdash{\Gamma_{0}}
holds in
MLL
.
We proceed by induction on a cut-free derivation of
\vdash{\Gamma_{0}}
,
resorting to the (well-known) cut-elimination theorem for
MLL
.

1.

l-ax
:

Then
\Gamma_{0}=A
and the derivation is of the form:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 13.72217pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\vphantom{{}^{@}}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=34.08325pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{l-ax}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\vdash{A^{\perp},A}$}}}}\end{array}

and we have

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 16.82336pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\vphantom{{}^{@}}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=40.28564pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-ax}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle A\vdash a:A$}}}}\end{array}

2.

l-
\otimes
:
Then
\Gamma_{0}
is a permutation of

\Gamma^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime\prime},B\otimes C

and the derivation is of the form:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\vdash{\Gamma^{\prime},B}\quad\vdash{\Gamma^{\prime\prime},C}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=70.25478pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{l-$\otimes$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 1.94449pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\vdash{\Gamma^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime\prime},B\otimes C}$}}}}\end{array}

Moreover, we know by hypothesis
that
\Gamma^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime\prime},B\otimes C

is a permutation of
\Gamma,A
.
We consider three subcases,
depending on whether
there is an occurrence of
A
in
\Gamma^{\prime}
,
or there is an occurrence of
A
in
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
,
or
A=B\otimes C
:

(a)

If there is an occurrence of
A
in
\Gamma^{\prime}
:
then
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma^{\prime\prime},A
and the derivation is of the form:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\vdash{\Gamma^{\prime\prime},A,B}\quad\vdash{\Gamma^{\prime\prime},C}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=84.50476pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{l-$\otimes$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 1.9445pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\vdash{\Gamma^{\prime\prime},A,\Gamma^{\prime\prime},B\otimes C}$}}}}\end{array}

Hence by IH there exist terms
s,r
such that:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\begin{array}[]{c}\text{IH }\\
\Gamma^{\prime\prime\perp},a:B^{\perp}\vdash s:A\end{array}\begin{array}[]{c}\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\begin{array}[]{c}\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 52.11568pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\vphantom{{}^{@}}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=110.87027pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-ax}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle b:B\multimap C^{\perp}\vdash b:B\multimap C^{\perp}$}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[]{c}\text{IH }\\
\Gamma^{\prime\prime\perp}\vdash r:C\end{array}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=204.29979pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-e$\multimap_{2}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 42.44571pt\hbox{$\displaystyle b:B\multimap C^{\perp},\Gamma^{\prime\prime\perp}\vdash b{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}r:B^{\perp}$}}}}\end{array}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=338.92918pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-sub}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 85.74768pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma^{\prime\prime\perp},\Gamma^{\prime\prime\perp},b:B\multimap C^{\perp}\vdash s{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}a:=b{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}r{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}:A$}}}}\end{array}

so it suffices to take
t:=s{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}a:=b{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}r{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}
.

(b)

If there is an occurrence of
A
in
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
:
then
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}=\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime},A
and the derivation is of the form

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\vdash{\Gamma,B}\quad\vdash{\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime},A,C}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=81.6992pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{l-$\otimes$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 1.94449pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\vdash{\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime},A,B\otimes C}$}}}}\end{array}

Hence by IH there exist terms
s,r
such that:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\begin{array}[]{c}\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\begin{array}[]{c}\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 52.11568pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\vphantom{{}^{@}}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=110.87027pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-ax}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle b:B\multimap C^{\perp}\vdash b:B\multimap C^{\perp}$}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[]{c}\text{IH }\\
\Gamma^{\perp}\vdash s:B\end{array}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=199.81113pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-e$\multimap_{1}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 41.00343pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma^{\perp},b:B\multimap C^{\perp}\vdash b\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,s:C^{\perp}$}}}}\end{array}\quad\begin{array}[]{c}\text{IH }\\
\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime\perp},c:C^{\perp}\vdash r:A\par\end{array}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=345.66531pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-sub}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 95.39352pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma^{\perp},\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime\perp},B\multimap C^{\perp}\vdash r{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}c:=b\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,s{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}:A$}}}}\end{array}

(c)

If
A=B\otimes C
and the derivation is of the form:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\vdash{\Gamma,B}\quad\vdash{\Gamma^{\prime\prime},C}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=67.44922pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{l-$\otimes$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 1.9445pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\vdash{\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime\prime},B\otimes C}$}}}}\end{array}

Hence by IH there exists terms
s,r
such that:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\begin{array}[]{c}\text{IH }\\
\Gamma^{\perp}\vdash s:B\end{array}\quad\begin{array}[]{c}\text{IH }\\
\Gamma^{\prime\prime\perp}\vdash r:C\end{array}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=118.48138pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-i$\otimes$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 11.94435pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma^{\perp},\Gamma^{\prime\prime\perp}\vdash\langle s,r\rangle:B\otimes C$}}}}\end{array}

3.

l-
\multimap
:
Then
\Gamma_{0}
is a permutation of
\Gamma^{\prime},B\multimap C
and the derivation is of the form

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 3.23608pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\vdash{\Gamma^{\prime},B^{\perp},C}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=55.00468pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{l-$\multimap$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\vdash{\Gamma^{\prime},B\multimap C}$}}}}\end{array}

Moreover, we know by hypothesis that
\Gamma^{\prime},B\multimap C
is a permutation of
\Gamma,A
.
We consider three subcases, depending on wheter there is an occurrence of
A
in
\Gamma^{\prime}
or
A=B\multimap C
.

(a)

If there is an occurrence of
A
in
\Gamma^{\prime}
:
then
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma^{\prime\prime},A
and the derivation is of the form:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 3.23607pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\vdash{\Gamma^{\prime\prime},A,B^{\perp},C}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=69.25464pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{l-$\multimap$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\vdash{\Gamma^{\prime\prime},A,B\multimap C}$}}}}\end{array}

and by IH we have:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\begin{array}[]{c}\text{IH }\\
\Gamma^{\prime\prime},b:B,c:C^{\perp}\vdash s:A\end{array}\quad\begin{array}[]{c}\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 48.16896pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\vphantom{{}^{@}}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=102.97684pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-ax}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle r:B\otimes C^{\perp}\vdash r:B\otimes C^{\perp}$}}}}\end{array}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=256.56578pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-e$\otimes$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 68.81586pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma^{\prime\prime},B\otimes C^{\perp}\vdash{s}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\langle b,c\rangle:=r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}:A$}}}}\end{array}

(b)

If
A=B\multimap C^{\perp}
and the derivation is of the form:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\vdash{\Gamma^{\prime},B^{\perp},C}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=48.53252pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{l-$\multimap$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 9.32184pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\vdash{\Gamma^{\prime},A}$}}}}\end{array}

Hence by IH we have that

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 0.6945pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\begin{array}[]{c}\text{IH }\\
\Gamma^{\prime},b:B\vdash t^{\prime}:C\end{array}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=77.10185pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-i$\multimap$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\lambda b.\,t^{\prime}:B\multimap C$}}}}\end{array}

∎

\ContrasubstitutionLemmaMLL

*

Proof.

We proceed by induction on the derivation of
\Gamma,a:A\vdash t:B
.

1.

m-ax
:

Then
\Gamma=\varnothing
,
t=a
and
A=B
.
Hence we can take

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 22.10756pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=44.21512pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash s:A^{\perp}$}}}}\end{array}

2.

m-i
\otimes
:
Then
t=\langle t_{1},t_{2}\rangle
and
B=B_{1}\otimes B_{2}
.
We consider two subcases, depending on whether

a\in\mathsf{fv}(t_{1})
or
a\in\mathsf{fv}(t_{2})
:

(a)

If
a\in\mathsf{fv}(t_{1})
:
then the derivation is of the form:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma_{1},a:A\vdash t_{1}:B_{1}\quad\Gamma_{2}\vdash t_{2}:B_{2}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=130.04254pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-i$\otimes$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 15.1961pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma_{1},a:A,\Gamma_{2}\vdash\langle t_{1},t_{2}\rangle:B$}}}}\end{array}

Furthermore, by hypothesis we have that

\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash s:B_{1}\multimap B_{2}^{\perp}
.
Recall that

\langle t_{1},t_{2}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}=t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}
.
Hence:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma_{1},a:A\vdash t_{1}:B_{1}\quad\begin{array}[]{c}\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash s:B_{1}\multimap B_{2}^{\perp}\quad\Gamma_{2}\vdash t_{2}:B_{2}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=127.11436pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-e$\multimap_{2}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 27.29506pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma_{2},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}t_{2}:B_{1}^{\perp}$}}}}\end{array}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=258.01466pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-cos}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 67.0901pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}t_{2}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}:A^{\perp}$}}}}\end{array}

(b)

If
a\in\mathsf{fv}(t_{2})
:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma_{1}\vdash t_{1}:B_{1}\quad\Gamma_{2},a:B_{2}\vdash t_{2}:B_{2}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=132.33775pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-i$\otimes$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 12.71178pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},a:A\vdash\langle t_{1},t_{2}\rangle:B_{2}$}}}}\end{array}

Furthermore, by hypothesis we have that
\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash s:B_{1}\multimap B_{2}^{\perp}
.
Recall that

\langle t_{1},t_{2}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}={t_{2}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash s\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}}
.
Hence:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\begin{array}[]{c}\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash s:B_{1}\multimap B_{2}\quad\Gamma_{1}\vdash t_{1}:A_{1}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=125.2637pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-e$\multimap_{1}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 24.70314pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma_{1},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash s\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,t_{1}:B^{\perp}$}}}}\end{array}\quad\Gamma_{2},a:A\vdash t_{2}:B_{2}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=256.164pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-cos}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 64.49818pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash t_{2}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash s\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}:A^{\perp}$}}}}\end{array}

3.

m-e
\otimes
: Then
t={t_{2}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\langle b,c\rangle:=t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}
. We consider two cases depending on whether
a\in\mathsf{fv}(t_{1})
or
a\in\mathsf{fv}(t_{2}
).

(a)

If
a\in\mathsf{fv}(t_{1})
, then the derivation is of the form:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma_{1},a:A\vdash t_{1}:C_{1}\otimes C_{2}\quad\Gamma_{2},b:C_{1},c:C_{2}\vdash t_{2}:B$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=206.55237pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-e$\otimes$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 39.28033pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma_{1},a:A,\Gamma_{2}\vdash{t_{2}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\langle b,c\rangle:=t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}:B$}}}}\end{array}

Furthermore, by hypothesis we have that
\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash s:B^{\perp}
.

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\begin{array}[]{c}\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\begin{array}[]{c}\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma_{2},b:C_{1},c:C_{2}\vdash t_{2}:B\quad\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash s:B^{\perp}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=155.03813pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-cos}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 23.19083pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma_{2},b:C_{1}\vdash t_{2}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}c\backslash\!\!\backslash s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}:C_{2}^{\perp}$}}}}\end{array}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=195.09372pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-i$\multimap$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 37.52934pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\par\Gamma_{2}\vdash\lambda b.\,s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}c\backslash\!\!\backslash B{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}:C_{1}\multimap C_{2}^{\perp}\par$}}}}\end{array}\quad{\Gamma_{1},a:A\vdash t_{1}:C_{1}\otimes C_{2}}\par\par$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=341.9655pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-cos}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 98.31148pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}\vdash t_{2}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash\lambda b.\,t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}c\backslash\!\!\backslash s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}:A^{\perp}$}}}}\end{array}

(b)

If
a\in\mathsf{fv}(t_{2})
, then the derivation is of the form:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma_{1}\vdash t_{1}:C_{1}\otimes C_{2}\quad\Gamma_{2},a:A,b:C_{1},c:C_{2}\vdash t_{2}:B$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=203.77466pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-e$\otimes$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 37.89148pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma_{1},a:A,\Gamma_{2}\vdash{t_{2}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\langle b,c\rangle:=t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}:B$}}}}\end{array}

Furthermore, by hypothesis we have that
\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash s:B^{\perp}
.

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma_{1}\vdash t_{1}:C_{1}\otimes C_{2}\quad\begin{array}[]{c}\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma_{2},b:B,c:C,a:A\vdash t_{2}:B\quad\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash s:B^{\perp}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=174.07587pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-cos}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 10.93489pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma_{2},\Gamma^{\prime},b:C_{1},c:C_{2}\vdash t_{2}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}:A^{\perp}$}}}}\end{array}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=292.10638pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-e$\otimes$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 68.85416pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash{t_{2}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\langle b,c\rangle:=t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}:A^{\perp}$}}}}\end{array}

4.

m-i
\multimap
:

Then
t=\lambda b.\,t^{\prime}
and
B=B_{1}\multimap B_{2}
.
The derivation is of the form:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 7.08336pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma,a:A,b:B_{1}\vdash t^{\prime}:B_{2}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=109.0564pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-i$\multimap$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma,a:A\vdash\lambda b.\,t^{\prime}:B_{1}\multimap B_{2}$}}}}\end{array}

Furthermore by hypothesis we have that
\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash s:B^{\perp}
.

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash s:B_{1}\otimes B_{2}^{\perp}\quad\begin{array}[]{c}\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma,a:A,b:B_{1}\vdash t^{\prime}:B_{2}\quad\begin{array}[]{c}\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 26.35866pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\vphantom{{}^{@}}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=59.35623pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-ax}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle c:B_{2}\vdash c:B_{2}$}}}}\end{array}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=201.19043pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-cos}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 33.64923pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma,b:B_{1},c:B_{2}^{\perp}\vdash t^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash c{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}:A^{\perp}$}}}}\end{array}\par$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=318.62085pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-e$\otimes$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 94.66986pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash{t^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash c{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\langle b,c\rangle:=s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}:A^{\perp}$}}}}\end{array}

5.

m-e
\multimap_{1}
.

Then
t=t_{1}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,t_{2}
.
We consider two cases depending on whether
a\in\mathsf{fv}(t_{1})
or
a\in\mathsf{fv}(t_{2}
).
Furthermore we know that
\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash s:B^{\perp}
.

(a)

If
a\in\mathsf{fv}(t_{1})
then the derivation is of the following form:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma_{1},a:A\vdash t_{1}:A\multimap B\quad\Gamma_{2}\vdash t_{2}:A$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=144.65007pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-e$\multimap_{1}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 34.72615pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}\vdash t_{1}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,t_{2}:B$}}}}\end{array}

Hence:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma_{1},a:A\vdash t_{1}:A\multimap B\quad\begin{array}[]{c}\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma_{2}\vdash t_{2}:A\quad\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash s:B^{\perp}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=95.57965pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-i$\otimes$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.5555pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma_{2},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\langle t_{2},s\rangle:A\otimes B^{\perp}$}}}}\end{array}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=236.67427pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-cos}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 55.031pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash\langle t_{2},s\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}:A^{\perp}$}}}}\end{array}

(b)

If
a\in\mathsf{fv}(t_{2})
then the derivation is of the following form:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma_{1}\vdash t_{1}:A\multimap B\quad\Gamma_{2},a:A\vdash t_{2}:A$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=144.65007pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-e$\multimap_{1}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 34.72615pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}\vdash t_{1}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,t_{2}:B$}}}}\end{array}

Hence:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\begin{array}[]{c}\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma_{1}\vdash t_{1}:A\multimap B\quad\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash s:B^{\perp}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=120.33302pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-e$\multimap_{2}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 22.80893pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma_{1},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}s:A^{\perp}$}}}}\end{array}\quad\Gamma_{2},a:A\vdash t_{2}:A$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=246.1604pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-cos}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 61.16298pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash t_{2}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}:A^{\perp}$}}}}\end{array}

6.

m-e
\multimap_{2}
:

Then
t=t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}t_{2}
and
B=C^{\perp}
.
We consider two cases depending on whether
a\in\mathsf{fv}(t_{1})
or
a\in\mathsf{fv}(t_{2}
).
Furthermore we know that
\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash s:C
.

(a)

If
a\in\mathsf{fv}(t_{1})
then the derivation is of the following form:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma_{1},a:A\vdash t_{1}:C\multimap A\quad\Gamma_{2}\vdash t_{2}:A^{\perp}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=150.17575pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-e$\multimap_{2}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 36.39276pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}\vdash t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}t_{2}:C^{\perp}$}}}}\end{array}

Recall that
(t_{1}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,t_{2}){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}=t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash\langle t_{2},s\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}
if
a\in\mathsf{fv}(t_{1})
.
Hence:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\begin{array}[]{c}\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma_{1},a:A\vdash t_{1}:C\multimap A\quad\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash s:C$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=142.47562pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-i$\multimap$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 20.91801pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma_{1},\Gamma^{\prime},a:A\vdash t_{1}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,s:A$}}}}\end{array}\quad\Gamma_{2}\vdash t_{2}:A^{\perp}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=239.55898pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-cos}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 56.47336pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash\langle t_{2},s\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}:A^{\perp}$}}}}\end{array}

(b)

If
a\in\mathsf{fv}(t_{2})
then the derivation is of the following form:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma_{1}\vdash t_{1}:C\multimap A\quad\Gamma_{2},a:A\vdash t_{2}:A^{\perp}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=150.17575pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-e$\multimap_{2}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 36.39276pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}\vdash t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}t_{2}:C^{\perp}$}}}}\end{array}

Hence:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\begin{array}[]{c}\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma_{1}\vdash t_{1}:C\multimap A\quad\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash s:C$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=114.13438pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-i$\multimap$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 20.91801pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma_{1},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash t_{1}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,s:A$}}}}\end{array}\quad\Gamma_{2},a:A\vdash t_{2}:A^{\perp}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=239.55898pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-cos}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 56.19566pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash t_{2}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash t_{1}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}:A^{\perp}$}}}}\end{array}

∎

Definition 26

(Size)
.

The size of a linear term
t
, denoted
\mathsf{sz}(t)
, is defined as

\begin{array}[]{rclcrcl}\mathsf{sz}(a)&:=&0&\mathsf{sz}(\langle t,s\rangle)&:=&1+\mathsf{sz}(t)+\mathsf{sz}(s)\\
\mathsf{sz}({s}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\langle a,b\rangle:=t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}})&:=&1+\mathsf{sz}(t)+\mathsf{sz}(s)&\mathsf{sz}(\lambda a.\,t)&:=&1+\mathsf{sz}(t)\\
\mathsf{sz}(t\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,s)&:=&1+\mathsf{sz}(t)+\mathsf{sz}(s)&\mathsf{sz}(t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}s)&:=&1+\mathsf{sz}(t)+\mathsf{sz}(s)\\
\end{array}

We extend this definition to case contexts as follows:

\displaystyle\mathsf{sz}(\Box):=0

\displaystyle\mathsf{sz}({\mathsf{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\langle a,b\rangle:=t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}):=\mathsf{sz}(\mathsf{L})+\mathsf{sz}(t)

Lemma 27
.

Let
t
be a linear term and
a\in\mathsf{fv}(t)
, then

\mathsf{sz}(t{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}a:=s{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}})=\mathsf{sz}(t)+\mathsf{sz}(s)
.

Proof.

By a straight-forward induction on the structure of the term
t
.
∎

Lemma 28
.

Let
t
be a linear term and
a\in\mathsf{fv}(t)
, then

\mathsf{sz}(t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}})=\mathsf{sz}(t)+\mathsf{sz}(s)
.

Proof.

By a straight-forward induction on the structure of the term
t
.
We show the case of the application
t=t_{1}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,t_{2}
, where
\mathsf{sz}(t)=\mathsf{sz}(t_{1})+\mathsf{sz}(t_{2})
.
The remaining cases follow by a similar reasoning.

1.

If
a\in\mathsf{fv}(t_{1})
then
(t_{1}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,t_{2}){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}=t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash\langle t_{2},s\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}
.
By IH,
\mathsf{sz}(t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash\langle t_{2},s\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}})=\mathsf{sz}(t_{1})+\mathsf{sz}(\langle t_{2},s\rangle)
.
Since
\mathsf{sz}(\langle t_{2},s\rangle)=\mathsf{sz}(t_{2})+\mathsf{sz}(s)+1
then this implies that
\mathsf{sz}((t_{1}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,t_{2}){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}})=\mathsf{sz}(t_{1})+\mathsf{sz}(t_{2})+\mathsf{sz}(s)+1=\mathsf{sz}(t_{1}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,t_{2})+\mathsf{sz}(s)
.

2.

If
a\in\mathsf{fv}(t_{2})
then
(t_{1}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,t_{2}){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}=t_{2}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}
.
By IH,
\mathsf{sz}(t_{2}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}})=\mathsf{sz}(t_{2})+\mathsf{sz}(t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}s)
.
Since
\mathsf{sz}(t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}s)=\mathsf{sz}(t_{2})+\mathsf{sz}(s)+1
then this implies that
\mathsf{sz}((t_{1}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,t_{2}){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}})=\mathsf{sz}(t_{2})+\mathsf{sz}(t_{1})+\mathsf{sz}(s)+1=\mathsf{sz}(t_{1}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,t_{2})+\mathsf{sz}(s)
.

∎

Lemma 29
.

\lambda_{\textsf{{MLL}}}
is strongly normalizing.

Proof.

We proceed by showing that each reduction rule strictly decreases the term’s size
i.e.
the size of the redex is strictly greater than the size of the contractum.
Since the size of a term is a non-negative integer, no infinite reduction sequences can exist.

Let
r
denote the redex and
r^{\prime}
the contractum; we proceed by case analysis of the three reductions rules.

1.

Case
(\lambda a.\,t)\mathsf{L}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,s\to t{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}a:=s{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}\mathsf{L}
.
The size of the redex is
\mathsf{sz}(r)=\mathsf{sz}((\lambda a.\,t)\mathsf{L}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,s)=\mathsf{sz}(\lambda a.\,t)+\mathsf{sz}(\mathsf{L})+\mathsf{sz}(s)=\mathsf{sz}(t)+1+\mathsf{sz}(\mathsf{L})+\mathsf{sz}(s)
.
For the contractum we have
\mathsf{sz}(r^{\prime})=\mathsf{sz}(t{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}a:=s{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}\mathsf{L})=\mathsf{sz}(t{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}a:=s{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}})+\mathsf{sz}(\mathsf{L})
, by Lem.
27
we get
\mathsf{sz}(t{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}a:=s{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}})+\mathsf{sz}(\mathsf{L})=\mathsf{sz}(t)+\mathsf{sz}(s)+\mathsf{sz}(\mathsf{L})
.
It follows that
\mathsf{sz}(r)=\mathsf{sz}(r^{\prime})+1
.

2.

Case
(\lambda a.\,t)\mathsf{L}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}s\to t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}\mathsf{L}
.
The size of the redex is
\mathsf{sz}(r)=\mathsf{sz}((\lambda a.\,t)\mathsf{L}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}s)=\mathsf{sz}(\lambda a.\,t)+\mathsf{sz}(\mathsf{L})+\mathsf{sz}(s)=\mathsf{sz}(t)+1+\mathsf{sz}(\mathsf{L})+\mathsf{sz}(s)
.
For the contractum we have
\mathsf{sz}(r^{\prime})=\mathsf{sz}(t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}\mathsf{L})=\mathsf{sz}(t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}})+\mathsf{sz}(\mathsf{L})
, by Lem.
28
we get
\mathsf{sz}(t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}})+\mathsf{sz}(\mathsf{L})=\mathsf{sz}(t)+\mathsf{sz}(s)+\mathsf{sz}(\mathsf{L})
.
It follows that
\mathsf{sz}(r)=\mathsf{sz}(r^{\prime})+1
.

3.

Case
{t}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\langle a,b\rangle:=\langle s,p\rangle\mathsf{L}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\to t{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}a:=s{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}b:=p{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}\mathsf{L}
.
The size of the redex is
\mathsf{sz}(r)=\mathsf{sz}({t}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\langle a,b\rangle:=\langle s,p\rangle\mathsf{L}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}})=\mathsf{sz}(t)+\mathsf{sz}(\langle s,p\rangle\mathsf{L})+1=\mathsf{sz}(t)+\mathsf{sz}(\langle s,p\rangle)+\mathsf{sz}(\mathsf{L})+1=\mathsf{sz}(t)+\mathsf{sz}(s)+\mathsf{sz}(p)+\mathsf{sz}(\mathsf{L})+1
.
For the contractum we have
\mathsf{sz}(r^{\prime})=\mathsf{sz}(t{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}a:=s{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}b:=p{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}\mathsf{L})=\mathsf{sz}(t{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}a:=s{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}b:=p{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}})+\mathsf{sz}(\mathsf{L})
, by Lem.
27
we get
\mathsf{sz}(t{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}a:=s{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}b:=p{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}})+\mathsf{sz}(\mathsf{L})=\mathsf{sz}(t)+\mathsf{sz}(s)+\mathsf{sz}(p)+\mathsf{sz}(\mathsf{L})
.
It follows that
\mathsf{sz}(r)=\mathsf{sz}(r^{\prime})+1
.

∎

Appendix B
A Contraposition-Based Calculus for
MELL

Lemma 30

(Weakening and Contraction)
.

1.

\Delta;\Gamma\vdash t:A
implies
\Delta,u:B;\Gamma\vdash t:A
.

2.

\Delta,u:B,v:B;\Gamma\vdash t:A
implies
\Delta,u:B;\Gamma\vdash t{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}v:=u{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}:A
.

Proof.

By induction on
\Delta;\Gamma\vdash t:A
, for the first item, and induction on
\Delta,u:B,v:B;\Gamma\vdash t:A
, for the second.
∎

Lemma 31

(Linear and unrestricted substitution are compatible with typing)
.

The following rules are admissible in
\lambda_{\textsf{{MELL}}}
:

\begin{array}[]{cc}\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Delta;\Gamma,a:A\vdash t:B\quad\Delta;\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash s:A$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=137.53188pt\hbox{\kern 3.06668pt$\textsc{{m-sub}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 21.38872pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Delta;\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash t{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}a:=s{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}:B$}}}}\end{array}&\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Delta,u:A;\Gamma\vdash t:B\quad\Delta;\cdot\vdash s:A$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=130.02615pt\hbox{\kern 3.06668pt$\textsc{{m-Usub}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 22.77765pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Delta;\Gamma\vdash t{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}u:=s{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}:B$}}}}\end{array}\end{array}

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of
\Delta;\Gamma,a:A\vdash t:B
, for the first item, and
\Delta,u:A;\Gamma\vdash t:B
for the second.
∎

It can be noted that the following two judgments are logically equivalent,
in the sense that the first one is provable if and only if the second
one is provable:

\Delta,u:A;\Gamma\vdash B\quad\quad\Delta;a:{!}{A},\Gamma\vdash B

More precisely, we have the following lemma:

Lemma 32
.

1.

If
\Delta,u:A;\Gamma\vdash t:B
,
then
\Delta;a:{!}{A},\Gamma\vdash t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}{!}{u}:=a{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}:B
.

2.

If
\Delta;a:{!}{A},\Gamma\vdash t:B
, then
\Delta,u:A;\Gamma\vdash t{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}a:={!}{b.(u\lightning b)}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}:B
,

Proof.

For the first item we have:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Delta;a:{!}{A}\vdash a:{!}{A}\quad\Delta,u:A;\Gamma\vdash t:B$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=150.63264pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-e${!}{}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 21.80545pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Delta;a:{!}{A},\Gamma\vdash t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}{!}{u}:=a{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}:B$}}}}\end{array}

For the second we have:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\begin{array}[]{c}\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 13.00111pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Delta;a:{!}{A},\Gamma\vdash t:B$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox to102.29128pt{$\mathord{=}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu=\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{=}$}\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{Lem.~\ref{lemma:weakening_and_contraction}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Delta,u:A;a:{!}{A},\Gamma\vdash t:B$}}}}\end{array}\quad\begin{array}[]{c}\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 15.54169pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Delta,u:A;\cdot\vdash u:A$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=101.42114pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-i${!}{}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Delta,u:A;\cdot\vdash{!}{b.(u\lightning b)}:{!}{A}$}}}}\end{array}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=293.84802pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-sub}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 74.45778pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Delta,u:A;\Gamma\vdash t{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}a:={!}{b.(u\lightning b)}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}:B$}}}}\end{array}

∎

Notation:
s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}{!}{\overline{u}}:=\overline{t}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}
to abbreviate

s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}{!}{u_{1}}:=t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\ldots{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}{!}{u_{n}}:=t_{n}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}
. Also,

t{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}\overline{a}:=\overline{s}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}
to abbreviate

t{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}a_{1}:=s_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}\ldots{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}a_{n}:=s_{n}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}
.

\SoundnessAndCompletenessOfCalcMELL

*

\Delta;\Gamma\vdash t:A

m-e
{?}
