Appendix C
Equivalence

C.1
Substitution and structural equivalence

Lemma 33
.

t\equiv s
implies
\mathsf{fv}(t)=\mathsf{fv}(s)

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of
t\equiv s
.
∎

Corollary 34
.

\equiv
is consistent.

Proof.

From Lem.
33
, we deduce that
a\equiv b
is not derivable, for arbitrary linear variables
a
and
b
such that
a\neq b
.
∎

Lemma 35

(Contrasubstitution is compatible with Equivalence (Right Case))
.

Let
t
be a linear term and
a\in\mathsf{fv}(t)
. Suppose
s\equiv r
. Then

t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}\equiv t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}
.

Proof.

By induction on
t
.

∎

We say that
r
is
contra-free for
a
in
t
, if the
free variables of
r
are not bound in

t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}
.

Lemma 36

(Eliminator flotation)
.

Let
t
be a linear term and
a\in\mathsf{fv}(t)
. Suppose
\mathsf{fv}(\mathtt{p})\cap\mathsf{fv}(t)=\varnothing
and
r
is contra-free for
a
in
t
. Then

t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\mathtt{p}:=r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\equiv t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\mathtt{p}:=r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}

Proof.

By induction on
t
.

∎

Lemma 37

(Eliminator flotation)
.

Let
t
be a linear term and
a\in\mathsf{fv}(t)
. Suppose
\mathsf{fv}(\mathtt{p})\cap\mathsf{fv}(t)=\varnothing
and
r
is free for
a
in
t
. Then

t{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}a:=s{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\mathtt{p}:=r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\equiv t{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}a:=s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\mathtt{p}:=r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}

Proof.

By induction on
t
.

∎

Lemma 38
.

Suppose
s\equiv r
. Then

1.

t{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}x:=s{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}\equiv s{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}x:=r{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}
; and

2.

s{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}x:=t{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}\equiv r{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}x:=t{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}
; and

Proof.

By induction on
t
.
∎

Remark 39
.

Since
\mathsf{fv}(\star)=\varnothing
we have:

1.

\langle t,s\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\equiv\langle t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}},s\rangle\equiv\langle t,s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\rangle
.

2.

(t\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,s){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\equiv t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,s\equiv t\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}
.

3.

(t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}s){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\equiv t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}s\equiv t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}
.

4.

(t\lightning s){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\equiv t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\lightning s\equiv t\lightning s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}
.

5.

({\invamp}(a,b).t){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\equiv{\invamp}(a,b).t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}
, if
a,b\notin\mathsf{flv}(r)
.

6.

t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\mathtt{p}:=s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\equiv t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\mathtt{p}:=s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\equiv t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\mathtt{p}:=s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}
.

7.

({?}u.t){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\equiv{?}u.t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}
, if
u\notin\mathsf{fv}(r)
.

Lemma 40

(Generalized Simpl)
.

Let
t
be a linear term and
a\in\mathsf{fv}(t)
. Then
t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash\star{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\equiv t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}

Proof.

By induction on
t
, using Lem.
36
and Lem.
35
and
\mathsf{symm}
.

∎

Lemma 41

(Contrasubstitution Lemma I)
.

Let
\{t,s,r\}
be a set of linear terms
such that
a\in\mathsf{fv}(t)
and let
b\neq a
.
Then

1.

t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}b:=r{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}=t{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}b:=r{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash s{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}b:=r{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}
.
Note that
b
occurs at most once in
t,s
,
so the right-hand side
is either of the form

t{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}b:=r{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}

or of the form

t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash s{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}b:=r{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}
.

2.

t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}u:=r{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}=t{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}u:=r{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash s{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}u:=r{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}
.

Proof.

By induction on
t
.

∎

Lemma 42

(Contra/contra lemma)
.

Let
\{t,s,r\}
be a linear set of terms
such that
a\in\mathsf{fv}(t)

and let
b\neq a

be such that
b\in\mathsf{fv}(t)\cup\mathsf{fv}(s)
.
Then:

1.

If
b\in\mathsf{fv}(t)
, then:

t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}b\backslash\!\!\backslash r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}\equiv t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}b\backslash\!\!\backslash s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}a:=r{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}

2.

If
b\in\mathsf{fv}(s)
, then:

t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}b\backslash\!\!\backslash r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}\equiv s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}b\backslash\!\!\backslash t{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}a:=r{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}

Proof.

By simulateneous induction on
t
.

∎

Lemma 43

(Nested Contra/contra lemma)
.

Let
\{t,s,r\}
be a linear set of terms
such that
a\in\mathsf{fv}(t)

and let
b\neq a

be such that
b\in\mathsf{fv}(s)
.
Then:

s{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}b:=t{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}\equiv t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}b\backslash\!\!\backslash r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}

Proof.

By induction on
s
.

∎

Lemma 44

(Contrasubstitution is compatible with equivalence)
.

Suppose
s\equiv r
. Then both
t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}\equiv t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}
and

s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}\equiv r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}
.

Proof.

The first item was proved in Lem.
35
. The second one can be proved by induction on the derivation of
s\equiv r
.

∎

C.2
An alternative presentation of
\equiv

This subsection provides an equivalent characterization of the structural equivalence, established in order to facilitate the proof of the strong bisimulation theorem (Thm.
14
).

Definition 45

(Alternative-structural equivalence)
.

The structural equivalence relation
\equiv^{\star}
is defined as the reflexive, symmetric, transitive and contextual closure of the following axioms and assumes that both sides are typed:

\begin{array}[]{llll}{\textsf{S}}\bm{\langle}{t}\bm{\rangle}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\mathtt{p}:=r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}&\equiv^{\star}_{\mathsf{Prop}}&{\textsf{S}}\bm{\langle}{t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\mathtt{p}:=r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}}\bm{\rangle}&(\mathsf{fv}(\mathtt{p})\cap\mathsf{fv}(\textsf{S})=\varnothing\mbox{ and }r\mbox{ free for }\textsf{S})\\
t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=\star{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}&\equiv^{\star}_{\beta\mathsf{\star}}&t\\
t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}&\equiv^{\star}_{\mathsf{\mathsf{Symm}\star}}&s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\\
\star\lightning t&\equiv^{\star}_{\mathsf{Lunit\_for\_\lightning}}&t&\\
(s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}t)\lightning r&\equiv^{\star}_{\mathsf{E\invamp_{2}/I\invamp}}&t\lightning s\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,r&\\
\langle r,t\rangle\lightning s&\equiv^{\star}_{\mathsf{I\otimes/E\invamp_{2}}}&r\lightning(s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}t)\\
{\invamp}(a,b).s\lightning t&\equiv^{\star}_{\mathsf{I\invamp/E\otimes}}&s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\langle a,b\rangle:=t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}&\\
({?}u.s)\lightning t&\equiv^{\star}_{\mathsf{I{?}/E{!}{}}}&s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}{!}{u}:=t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\\
({!}{a.t})\lightning s&\equiv^{\star}_{\mathsf{I{!}{}/E{?}}}&{t{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\blacktriangleright}^{a}s}&\\
t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\lightning r&\equiv^{\star}_{\mathsf{I\perp/E\star}}&s\lightning(r\lightning t)&\end{array}

Lemma 46
.

The following equations are in
\equiv^{\star}

\begin{array}[]{llll}\star\lightning t&\equiv^{\star}_{\mathsf{Lunit\_for\_\lightning}}&t&\\
{\textsf{S}}\bm{\langle}{t}\bm{\rangle}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\mathtt{p}:=r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}&\equiv^{\star}_{\mathsf{Prop}}&{\textsf{S}}\bm{\langle}{t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\mathtt{p}:=r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}}\bm{\rangle}&\mbox{ if }\mathsf{fv}(\mathtt{p})\cap\mathsf{fv}(\textsf{S})=\varnothing\mbox{ and }r\mbox{ free for }\textsf{S}\\
t\lightning s&\equiv^{\star}_{\mathsf{Symm\_for\_\lightning}}&s\lightning t&\\
(s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}t)\lightning r&\equiv^{\star}_{\mathsf{E\invamp_{2}/I\otimes}}&s\lightning\langle r,t\rangle&\\
(s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}t)\lightning r&\equiv^{\star}_{\mathsf{E\invamp_{2}/I\invamp}}&t\lightning s\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,r&\\
(s\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,t)\lightning r&\equiv^{\star}_{\mathsf{E\invamp_{1}/E\invamp_{2}}}&t\lightning s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}r&\\
\langle t,s\rangle\lightning r&\equiv^{\star}_{\mathsf{E\otimes/E\invamp_{1}}}&s\lightning(r\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,t)&\\
\langle t,s\rangle\lightning r&\equiv^{\star}_{\mathsf{I\otimes/E\invamp_{2}}}&t\lightning(r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}s)&\\
{\invamp}(a,b).s\lightning t&\equiv_{\mathsf{I\invamp/E\otimes}}&s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\langle a,b\rangle:=t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}&\\
({?}u.s)\lightning t&\equiv^{\star}_{\mathsf{I{?}/E{!}{}}}&s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}{!}{u}:=t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}&\\
({!}{a.t}){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=p{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\lightning r&\equiv^{\star}_{\mathsf{I{!}{}/E{?}}}&p\lightning{t{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\blacktriangleright}^{a}r}&\\
t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\lightning r&\equiv^{\star}_{\mathsf{I\perp/E\star}}&s\lightning(r\lightning t)&\end{array}

Proof.

We give the proof for the rule
ruleCCEqOneLUnitForBott
, the remaining rules follow from similar reasonings.

\begin{array}[]{llll}&t\lightning s\\
\equiv^{\star}&t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=\star{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\lightning s&\beta\mathsf{\star}\\
\equiv^{\star}&\star\lightning(s\lightning t)&\mathsf{I\perp/E\star}\\
\equiv^{\star}&s\lightning t&\mathsf{Lunit\_for\_\lightning}\\
\end{array}

∎

We establish several auxiliar results to show that the relation
\equiv^{\star}
coincides with
\equiv
.
The only challenge lies in proving that
\equiv^{\star}
captures
\mathsf{split}
, the fourth axiom of
\equiv
(Def.
13
).
The remaining axioms
\mathsf{Prop}
,
\beta\mathsf{\star}
, and
\mathsf{\mathsf{Symm}\star}
are common to both relations.
To this end, we begin by defining the notions of a deep context and the dual of a deep context.

Definition 47
.

Deep contexts

P
are defined by the grammar:

\begin{array}[]{lll}\textsf{P}&::=&\Box\,|\,{\invamp}(a,b).\textsf{P}\,|\,\textsf{P}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}t\,|\,t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}\textsf{P}\,|\,\textsf{P}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,t\,|\,t\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,\textsf{P}\,|\,\langle\textsf{P},t\rangle\\
&&|\,\langle t,\textsf{P}\rangle\,|\,\textsf{P}\lightning t\,|\,t\lightning\textsf{P}\,|\,{?}u.\textsf{P}\,|\,\textsf{P}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\mathtt{p}:=t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\,|\,t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\mathtt{p}:=\textsf{P}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\,|\,{t{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\blacktriangleright}^{a}\textsf{P}}\end{array}

We say
t
is free for
P
if the free variables of
t
are not captured in
{\textsf{P}}\bm{\langle}{t}\bm{\rangle}
.
Similarly, a
context
P
is free for a deep context
Q
if
{\textsf{Q}}\bm{\langle}{\textsf{P}}\bm{\rangle}
does not bind free occurrences of variables of
P
.

Definition 48

(Dual of a deep context)
.

The dual of a deep context
P
, denoted
\textsf{P}^{\perp}
, is defined as the deep context
\textsf{P}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}\Box\backslash\!\!\backslash\Box{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}
.

Lemma 49

(Contrasubstitution lemma)
.

Suppose
t
is free for
P
. Then
{\textsf{P}}\bm{\langle}{a}\bm{\rangle}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}\equiv{\textsf{P}^{\perp}}\bm{\langle}{t}\bm{\rangle}
.

Proof.

By induction on
P
.

∎

Lemma 50

(Dual Contravariant on Deep Context Composition)
.

Suppose
Q
is free for
P
. Then
{\textsf{P}}\bm{\langle}{\textsf{Q}}\bm{\rangle}^{\perp}\equiv{\textsf{Q}^{\perp}}\bm{\langle}{\textsf{P}^{\perp}}\bm{\rangle}

The following example illustrates why the condition that
Q
be free for
P
is required.
Suppose
\textsf{P}={\invamp}(c,d).\Box
and
\textsf{Q}=\Box{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\langle a,b\rangle:=\langle c,d\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}
. Then

\begin{array}[]{l}{\textsf{P}}\bm{\langle}{\textsf{Q}}\bm{\rangle}={\invamp}(c,d).\Box{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\langle a,b\rangle:=\langle c,d\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\\
{\textsf{P}}\bm{\langle}{\textsf{Q}}\bm{\rangle}^{\perp}=\star{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\langle a,b\rangle:=\langle c,d\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\langle c,d\rangle:=\Box{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\\
\textsf{P}^{\perp}=\star{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\langle c,d\rangle:=\Box{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\\
\textsf{Q}^{\perp}=\Box{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\langle a,b\rangle:=\langle c,d\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\\
{\textsf{Q}^{\perp}}\bm{\langle}{\textsf{P}^{\perp}}\bm{\rangle}=\star{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\langle c,d\rangle:=\Box{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\langle a,b\rangle:=\langle c,d\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\\
\end{array}

Notice that

\begin{array}[]{l}{\textsf{P}}\bm{\langle}{\textsf{Q}}\bm{\rangle}^{\perp}=\star{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\langle a,b\rangle:=\langle c,d\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\langle c,d\rangle:=\Box{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\not\equiv\star{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\langle c,d\rangle:=\Box{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\langle a,b\rangle:=\langle c,d\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}={\textsf{Q}^{\perp}}\bm{\langle}{\textsf{P}^{\perp}}\bm{\rangle}\end{array}

Proof.

By induction on
P
.

∎

We are ready to prove that
\mathsf{split}
is captured by
\equiv^{\star}
(Lem.
51
).

Lemma 51

(Derivability of
\mathsf{split}
)
.

{\textsf{P}^{\perp}}\bm{\langle}{\star}\bm{\rangle}\lightning r\equiv^{\star}{\textsf{P}}\bm{\langle}{r}\bm{\rangle}
, if
r
is free for
P
.

Proof.

By induction on
P
, “inside out” using Lem.
50
, Lem.
36
, and Lem.
46
. In other words, we consider each possible case for the innermost term constructor used in constructing
P
.

∎

Proposition 52
.

\equiv=\equiv^{\star}
.

Proof.

We prove that
\equiv\subseteq\equiv^{\star}
and then that
\equiv^{\star}\subseteq\equiv
. We do so by showing that the axioms of

\equiv
are provable in
\equiv^{\star}
, for the first item, and then that the axioms of
\equiv^{\star}
are provable in
\equiv
, for the second one.

1.

\equiv\subseteq\equiv^{\star}
.

For the first item we note the following:

1.1.

\mathsf{Prop},\beta\mathsf{\star}
, and
\mathsf{\mathsf{Symm}\star}
.

\begin{array}[]{llll}{\textsf{S}}\bm{\langle}{t}\bm{\rangle}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\mathtt{p}:=r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}&\equiv_{\mathsf{Prop}}&{\textsf{S}}\bm{\langle}{t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\mathtt{p}:=r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}}\bm{\rangle}\\
t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=\star{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}&\equiv_{\beta\mathsf{\star}}&t\\
t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}&\equiv_{\mathsf{\mathsf{Symm}\star}}&s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\end{array}

where, in axiom
\mathsf{Prop}
,
\mathsf{fv}(\mathtt{p})\cap\mathsf{fv}(\textsf{S})=\varnothing
and
r
free for
S
. All three axioms are included in
\equiv^{\star}
, so we conclude immediately.

1.2.

\mathsf{split}
.

t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash\star{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}\lightning r\equiv_{\mathsf{split}}t{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}a:=r{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}

This axiom is provable in
\equiv^{\star}
, as follows from Lem.
49
and Lem.
51
.

2.

\equiv^{\star}\subseteq\equiv
.

We now consider the second item. Once again, axioms
\mathsf{Prop},\beta\mathsf{\star}
, and
\mathsf{split}
are all included in
\equiv
. So it remains to show that the others are provable in
\equiv
.

2.1.

t\equiv\star\lightning t

\begin{array}[]{llll}&t\\
\equiv&t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=\star{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}&\beta\mathsf{\star}\\
=&a{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=\star{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}a:=t{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}\\
\equiv&a{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=\star{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash\star{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}\lightning t&\mathsf{split}\\
=&\star{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=\star{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\lightning t&Def.~\ref{def:cos}\\
\equiv&\star\lightning t&\beta\mathsf{\star}\end{array}

2.2.

(s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}t)\lightning r\equiv t\lightning(s\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,r)

\begin{array}[]{llll}&(s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}t)\lightning r\\
=&((s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}a)\lightning r){\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}a:=t{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}\\
\equiv&((s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}a)\lightning r){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash\star{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}\lightning t&\mathsf{split}\\
=&(s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}a){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=\star{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}\lightning t\\
=&(s\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=\star{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}})\lightning t\\
\equiv&(s\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,r)\lightning t&\beta\mathsf{\star}\\
\equiv&t\lightning(s\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,r)&\mathsf{Symm\_for\_\lightning}(Lem.~\ref{lemma:cceq_derived})\\
\end{array}

2.3.

\langle r,t\rangle\lightning s\equiv r\lightning(s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}t)

\begin{array}[]{llll}&\langle r,t\rangle\lightning s&\\
=&\langle r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=\star{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}},t\rangle\lightning s&\beta\mathsf{\star}\\
=&(a{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}t){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=\star{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}\lightning s&Def.~\ref{def:cos}\\
\equiv&((a{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}t)\lightning r){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash\star{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}\lightning s&Def.~\ref{def:cos}\\
=&((a{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}t)\lightning r){\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}a:=s{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}&\mathsf{split}\\
=&(s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}t)\lightning r\\
\end{array}

2.4.

({\invamp}(a,b).s)\lightning t\equiv_{\mathsf{I\invamp/E\otimes}}s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\langle a,b\rangle:=t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}

\begin{array}[]{llll}&({\invamp}(a,b).s)\lightning t\\
=&({\invamp}(a,b).(s\lightning\star))\lightning t&\mathsf{Lunit\_for\_\lightning}\\
=&(({\invamp}(a,b).(s\lightning c))\lightning t){\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}c:=\star{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}\\
\equiv&(({\invamp}(a,b).(s\lightning c))\lightning t){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}c\backslash\!\!\backslash\star{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}\lightning\star&\mathsf{split}\\
\equiv&(({\invamp}(a,b).(s\lightning c))\lightning t){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}c\backslash\!\!\backslash\star{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}&\mathsf{Lunit\_for\_\lightning}\\
=&({\invamp}(a,b).(s\lightning c)){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}c\backslash\!\!\backslash t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=\star{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}&Def.~\ref{def:cos}\\
\equiv&({\invamp}(a,b).(s\lightning c)){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}c\backslash\!\!\backslash t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}&\beta\mathsf{\star}\\
=&(s\lightning c){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}c\backslash\!\!\backslash\star{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\langle a,b\rangle:=t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}&Def.~\ref{def:cos}\\
\equiv&c{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}c\backslash\!\!\backslash s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=\star{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\langle a,b\rangle:=t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}{r}&Lem.~\ref{lemma:contra_contra_lemma}\\
=&s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=\star{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\langle a,b\rangle:=t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}&Def.~\ref{def:cos}\\
=&s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\langle b,c\rangle:=t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}&\beta\mathsf{\star}\\
\end{array}

2.5.

({?}u.s)\lightning t\equiv s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}{!}{u}:=t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}

\begin{array}[]{llll}&({?}u.s)\lightning t\\
\equiv&({?}u.s\lightning\star)\lightning t\\
=&(({?}u.s\lightning a)\lightning t){\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}a:=r{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}\\
\equiv&(({?}u.s\lightning a)\lightning t){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash\star{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}\lightning\star&\mathsf{split}\\
\equiv&(({?}u.s\lightning a)\lightning t){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash\star{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}&\\
=&({?}u.s\lightning a){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=\star{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}&Def.~\ref{def:cos}\\
=&({?}u.s\lightning a){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}\\
=&(s\lightning a){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash\star{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}{!}{u}:=t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}&Def.~\ref{def:cos}\\
=&a{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=\star{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}{!}{u}:=t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}&Def.~\ref{def:cos}\\
\equiv&s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}{!}{u}:=t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}&\beta\mathsf{\star}\\
\end{array}

2.6.

({!}{a.t})\lightning s\equiv{t{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\blacktriangleright}^{a}s}

\begin{array}[]{llll}&{t{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\blacktriangleright}^{a}s}\\
=&({t{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\blacktriangleright}^{a}b}){\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}b:=s{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}\\
\equiv&({t{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\blacktriangleright}^{a}b}){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}b\backslash\!\!\backslash\star{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}\lightning s&{t{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\blacktriangleright}^{a}b}:{\perp},\mathsf{split}\\
=&b{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}b\backslash\!\!\backslash{!}{a.t}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=\star{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\lightning s\\
=&({!}{a.t}){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=\star{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\lightning s\\
\equiv&({!}{a.t})\lightning s\\
\end{array}

2.7.

t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\lightning r\equiv s\lightning(r\lightning t)

\begin{array}[]{llll}&s\lightning(r\lightning t)\\
\equiv&(t\lightning r)\lightning s&\mathsf{Symm\_for\_\lightning}(Lem.~\ref{lemma:cceq_derived})\\
\equiv&(t\lightning r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=\star{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}})\lightning s&\beta\mathsf{\star}\\
=&a{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash t\lightning r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=\star{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}\lightning s&Def.~\ref{def:cos}\\
=&t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=a{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=\star{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}\lightning s&Def.~\ref{def:cos}\\
=&(t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=a{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\lightning r){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash\star{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}\lightning s&Def.~\ref{def:cos}\\
\equiv&(t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=a{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\lightning r){\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}a:=s{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}&\mathsf{split}\\
=&t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\lightning r\\
\end{array}

∎

\StrongBisimulation

*

Proof.

We proceed by induction on the derivation of
t\equiv^{\star}s
.
We first demonstrate the result for the 10 axioms by performing a case analysis on the positions of the redexes within the terms.
We then show that strong bisimulation is preserved under the inference rules.

∎

\SubjectReduction

*

Proof.

Note that structural equivalence relates terms of the same type and under the same contexts, by definition. Thus we are left to verify that
\Delta;\Gamma\vdash t:A
and
t\to_{\mathsf{\lambda_{\textsf{{MELL}}}}}^{-}s
implies
\Delta;\Gamma\vdash s:A
. This is proved by induction on
t\to_{\mathsf{\lambda_{\textsf{{MELL}}}}}^{-}s
.
∎

Appendix D
Strong Normalization

Lemma 53

(Dual Properties)
.

Let
A
be a type and
X,Y\subseteq{\cal T}_{A}
then the following properties hold.

1.

If
X\subseteq Y
then
Y^{\perp}\subseteq X^{\perp}
.

2.

X\subseteq X^{\perp}{}^{\perp}
.

3.

X^{\perp}=X^{\perp}{}^{\perp}{}^{\perp}
.

Proof.

Let’s prove each of the properties.

1.

If
t\in Y^{\perp}
then
\forall s\in Y
we have that
t\lightning s\vartriangleleft_{\bot}\textrm{SN}_{\bot}
.
In particular since
X\subseteq Y
then this implies that
\forall s\in X
we have that
t\lightning s\in\textrm{SN}_{\bot}
.

2.

By the definition we have that

X^{\perp}{}^{\perp}=\{r\in{\cal T}_{A}:\forall s\in X^{\perp},\ r\lightning s\vartriangleleft_{\bot}\textrm{SN}_{\bot}.\}

Let
t\in X
we have that
\forall s\in X^{\perp}
this implies that
s\lightning t\vartriangleleft_{\bot}\textrm{SN}_{\bot}
by the definition of the dual set
X^{\perp}
.
Since
s\lightning t\equiv t\lightning s
, this implies that
t\in X^{\perp}{}^{\perp}
.

3.

This follows from the previous properties.
By the property
2
we have that
X\subseteq X^{\perp}{}^{\perp}
and by the property
1
we have that
X^{\perp}{}^{\perp}{}^{\perp}\subseteq X^{\perp}
.
Then by the property
2
we have that
X^{\perp}\subseteq(X^{\perp})^{\perp}{}^{\perp}=X^{\perp}{}^{\perp}{}^{\perp}
thus proving that
X^{\perp}=X^{\perp}{}^{\perp}{}^{\perp}
.

∎

Proposition 54
.

Let
A
be a type then:

1.

For all
u
unrestricted variables we have that
u\in\llbracket A\rrbracket
.

2.

\llbracket A\rrbracket\subseteq\textrm{SN}_{A}
.

Proof.

We prove this by mutual induction on the type
A
.

•

If
A=\alpha,\alpha^{\perp},\bot,\text{1}
then for any unrestricted variable we have that
u\in\textrm{SN}_{A}
.
By definition of the reducibility candidates we have in this particular case that
\llbracket A\rrbracket=\textrm{SN}_{A}
.

•

If
A=A_{1}\otimes A_{2}
then by the inductive hypothesis we have that there are unrestricted variables
u_{1},u_{2}
such that
u_{1}\in\llbracket A_{1}\rrbracket,u_{2}\in\llbracket A_{2}\rrbracket
then by the lemma
63
we have that the set
\llbracket A_{1}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket A_{2}\rrbracket\subseteq\textrm{SN}_{A_{1}\otimes A_{2}}
and that
\llbracket A_{1}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket A_{2}\rrbracket\neq\varnothing
.
Thus since
\llbracket A_{1}\otimes A_{2}\rrbracket=(\llbracket A_{1}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket A_{2}\rrbracket)^{\perp}{}^{\perp}
by the lemma
62
applied twice we conclude what we wanted to prove.

•

If
A=A_{1}\invamp A_{2}
then by the inductive hypothesis, for all unrestricted variables
u
we have that
u\in\llbracket A_{1}^{\perp}\otimes A_{2}^{\perp}\rrbracket
.
Thus since
\llbracket A_{1}\invamp A_{2}\rrbracket=\llbracket A_{1}^{\perp}\otimes A_{2}^{\perp}\rrbracket^{\perp}
then by the lemma
62
we get what we wanted to prove.

•

If
A={?}A_{1}
then by the inductive hypothesis, for all unrestricted variables
u
we have that
u\in\llbracket A^{\perp}\rrbracket
.
This implies that by the lemma
64
the following holds
{?}\llbracket A_{1}^{\perp}\rrbracket\subseteq\textrm{SN}_{{?}A_{1}}
and
{?}\llbracket A_{1}^{\perp}\rrbracket\neq\varnothing
.
Thus since
\llbracket{?}A_{1}\rrbracket=({?}\llbracket A_{1}^{\perp}\rrbracket)^{\perp}{}^{\perp}
then by the lemma
62
applied twice we conclude what we wanted to prove.

•

If
A={!}{A_{1}}
then by the inductive hypothesis, for all unrestricted variables
u
we have that
u\in\llbracket{?}A_{1}^{\perp}\rrbracket
.
Thus since
\llbracket{!}{A_{1}}\rrbracket=\llbracket{?}A_{1}^{\perp}\rrbracket^{\perp}
by the lemma
62
we get what we wanted to prove.

∎

Definition 55
.

Given
A
a type,
X\subseteq\textrm{SN}_{A}
we say that
X
is
reduction closed
if for all
t\in X
and
t^{\prime}
such that
t\to_{\mathsf{\lambda_{\textsf{{MELL}}}}}^{-}t^{\prime}
we have that
t^{\prime}\in X
.

Lemma 56
.

If
A
is a type then
\textrm{SN}_{A}
is reduction closed.

Proof.

If
t\in\textrm{SN}_{A}
then for all reducts
t^{\prime}
we have that
t^{\prime}\in{\cal T}_{A}
by subject reduction and since
t
is strongly normalizing we get that
t^{\prime}
is strongly normalizing too.
We conclude that
t^{\prime}\in\textrm{SN}_{A}
.
∎

Lemma 57
.

If
A
is a type and
X\subseteq\textrm{SN}_{A}
then
X^{\perp}
is reduction closed.

Proof.

If
t\in X^{\perp}
then for all
s\in X
we have that
t\lightning s\in\textrm{SN}_{\bot}
, in particular this implies that
t\in\textrm{SN}_{A^{\perp}}
.
If
t\to_{\mathsf{\lambda_{\textsf{{MELL}}}}}^{-}t^{\prime}
then by subject reduction
t^{\prime}\lightning s\in{\cal T}_{\bot}
and moreover
t^{\prime}\lightning s\in\textrm{SN}_{\bot}
since it is a reduct of
t\lightning s
.
∎

Proposition 58
.

For all types
A
we have that
\llbracket A\rrbracket
is reduction closed.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on the type
A
.

If
A=\alpha,\alpha^{\perp},\bot,\text{1}
then this follows from the lemma
56
.

If
A=A_{1}\otimes A_{2},A_{1}\invamp A_{2},{?}A_{1},{!}{A_{1}}
then this follows from the induction hypothesis and from the application of the lemma
57

∎

Lemma 59

(Adequacy for
\invamp
)
.

If
\Delta;\Gamma,a_{1}:A_{1},a_{2}:A_{2}\vdash t:\bot
such that

t\in\llbracket\bot\rrbracket
and for all
s_{1}\in\llbracket A_{1}\rrbracket,s_{2}\in\llbracket A_{2}\rrbracket
we get that
t{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}a_{1}:=s_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}a_{2}:=s_{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}\in\llbracket\bot\rrbracket
then this implies that
{\invamp}(a_{1},a_{2}).t\in\llbracket A_{1}^{\perp}\invamp A_{2}^{\perp}\rrbracket
.

Proof.

Since
\llbracket A_{1}^{\perp}\invamp A_{2}^{\perp}\rrbracket=\llbracket A_{1}^{\perp}\otimes A_{2}^{\perp}\rrbracket^{\perp}=(\llbracket A_{1}^{\perp}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket A_{2}^{\perp}\rrbracket)^{\perp}
it suffices to prove that for any
\langle r_{1},r_{2}\rangle\in(\llbracket A_{1}^{\perp}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket A_{2}^{\perp}\rrbracket)^{\perp}
we have that
({\invamp}(a_{1},a_{2}).t)\lightning\langle r_{1},r_{2}\rangle\in\llbracket\bot\rrbracket
.
Observe that the terms
t
,
r_{1}
and
r_{2}
are strongly normalizing by the proposition
54
.
This means that the natural number
|t|+|r_{1}|+|r_{2}|
is well defined.

We prove this by induction on
|t|+|r_{1}|+|r_{2}|
.
The base case is straightforward.
For the induction step we consider three different cases depending on the reduction step.

1.

If
t\to t^{\prime}
,
this follows straight from the inductive hypothesis.

2.

If
r_{1}\to r_{1}^{\prime}
or
r_{2}\to r_{2}^{\prime}
,
this follows straight from the inductive hypothesis.

3.

If
r=\langle r_{1},r_{2}\rangle
and
{\invamp}(a_{1},a_{2}).t\lightning\langle r_{1},r_{2}\rangle\to t{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}a_{1}:=r_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}a_{2}:=r_{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}
then this follows from the hypothesis.

∎

The following result is proved by induction on
A
.

Lemma 60
.

For all types
A
the following equality holds

\llbracket A^{\perp}\rrbracket=\llbracket A\rrbracket^{\perp}

Proof.

We proceed by induction on the type
A
.

•

If
A=\alpha,\alpha^{\perp},\text{1},\bot
then we must prove that
\textrm{SN}_{A}^{\perp}=\textrm{SN}_{A^{\perp}}
.

If
t\in\textrm{SN}_{A}^{\perp}
then for all
s\in\textrm{SN}_{A}
we have that
t\lightning s\vartriangleleft_{\bot}\textrm{SN}_{\bot}
then this implies that
t\in{\cal T}_{A^{\perp}}
and that
t\in\textrm{SN}_{A^{\perp}}
in particular.
If
t\in\textrm{SN}_{A^{\perp}}
then we must prove that for all
s\in\textrm{SN}_{A}
we have that
t\lightning s\vartriangleleft_{\bot}\textrm{SN}_{\bot}
.
Observe that because of the types considered, the term
t\lightning s
cannot have a root redex and since both terms
t,s
are strongly normalizing then this implies that
t\lightning s\vartriangleleft_{\bot}\textrm{SN}_{\bot}
.

•

If
A=A_{1}\otimes A_{2}
then
(A_{1}\otimes A_{2})^{\perp}=A_{1}^{\perp}\invamp A_{2}^{\perp}
and we have that

\llbracket A_{1}^{\perp}\invamp A_{2}^{\perp}\rrbracket=(\llbracket A_{1}^{\perp}{}^{\perp}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket A_{2}^{\perp}{}^{\perp}\rrbracket)^{\perp}=\llbracket A_{1}\otimes A_{2}\rrbracket^{\perp}

•

If
A=A_{1}\invamp A_{2}
then
(A_{1}\invamp A_{2})^{\perp}=A_{1}^{\perp}\otimes A_{2}^{\perp}
and we have that using the definition of reducibility candidates and the lemma
53
the following is valid:

\llbracket A_{1}^{\perp}\otimes A_{2}^{\perp}\rrbracket=\llbracket A_{1}^{\perp}\otimes A_{2}^{\perp}\rrbracket^{\perp}{}^{\perp}=((\llbracket A_{1}^{\perp}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket A_{2}^{\perp}\rrbracket)^{\perp}{}^{\perp})^{\perp}{}^{\perp}=\llbracket A_{1}\invamp A_{2}\rrbracket^{\perp}

•

If
A={?}A_{1}
then
({?}A_{1})^{\perp}={!}{A_{1}^{\perp}}
and we have that
\llbracket{{?}A_{1}}\rrbracket^{\perp}=\llbracket{!}{A_{1}^{\perp}}\rrbracket
.

•

If
A={!}{A_{1}}
then
({!}{A_{1}})^{\perp}={?}A_{1}^{\perp}
and we have that using the definition of reducibility candidates and the lemma
53
the following is valid:

\llbracket{!}{A_{1}}\rrbracket^{\perp}=\llbracket{?}A_{1}^{\perp}\rrbracket^{\perp}{}^{\perp}=({?}\llbracket{A_{1}}\rrbracket^{\perp}{}^{\perp})^{\perp}{}^{\perp}=\llbracket{?}A_{1}\rrbracket^{\perp}

∎

Corollary 61
.

For all types
A
the following equality holds

\llbracket A\rrbracket=\llbracket A\rrbracket^{\perp}{}^{\perp}

Proof.

This follows from the fact that for all types
A^{\perp}{}^{\perp}=A
and from applying the last lemma
60
to
A^{\perp}
.
∎

Note that for any type
A
we have that any unrestricted variable
u
is such that
u\in\textrm{SN}_{A}
.

Lemma 62
.

Let
X\subseteq\textrm{SN}_{A}
such that
X\neq\varnothing
then:

1.

If
u
is an unrestricted variable then
u\in X^{\perp}
.

2.

X^{\perp}\subseteq\textrm{SN}_{A^{\perp}}
.

Proof.

For the item
1
.
Let
t\in X
then for
u\in\textrm{SN}_{A^{\perp}}
we have that
t\lightning u\vartriangleleft_{\bot}\textrm{SN}_{\bot}
since there is not a root redex and both
t
and
u
are strongly normalizing terms.

For the item
2
.
Let
s\in X^{\perp}
then for any
t\in X
we have that
s\lightning t\vartriangleleft_{\bot}\textrm{SN}_{\bot}
and this implies that
s\in\textrm{SN}_{A^{\perp}}
.
∎

Lemma 63
.

Let
X\subseteq\textrm{SN}_{A},Y\subseteq\textrm{SN}_{B}
such that
u\in X
and
v\in Y
for all
u,v
unrestricted variables then:

1.

X\otimes Y\neq\varnothing

2.

X\otimes Y\subseteq\textrm{SN}_{A\otimes B}
.

Proof.

For the item
1
.
We have that
\langle u,v\rangle\in{\cal T}_{A\otimes B}
thus
X\otimes Y\neq\varnothing
.

For the item
2
.
For all terms
\langle t,s\rangle\in X\otimes Y
we have that since both
t,s
are strongly normalizing terms then
\langle t,s\rangle
is strongly normalizing too.
∎

Lemma 64
.

Let
X\subseteq\textrm{SN}_{A}
such that
u\in X
for all
u
unrestricted variable then:

1.

{?}X\neq\varnothing

2.

{?}X\subseteq\textrm{SN}_{{?}A^{\perp}}
.

Proof.

For the item
1
.
Since
X\subseteq\textrm{SN}_{A}
then for all
s\in X
we have that
v{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}w:=s{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}=v
thus
v\in{?}X
.

For the item
2
.
If
{?}u.t\in{?}X
then since
u\in X
we have that
t{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}u:=u{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}\in\textrm{SN}_{\bot}
thus
t\in\textrm{SN}_{\bot}
and this implies that
{?}u.t\in\textrm{SN}_{{?}A^{\perp}}
.

∎

Lemma 65
.

Let
t\in{\cal T}_{\bot}
and judgement
\Delta;a:A,\Gamma\vdash t:\bot
such that for all
s\in\llbracket A\rrbracket
we have that
t{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}a:=s{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}\in\llbracket\bot\rrbracket
then this implies that
t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash\ast{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}\in\llbracket A^{\perp}\rrbracket
.

Proof.

If
r\in\llbracket A\rrbracket
then using the structural equivalence

t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}\lightning r\equiv t{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}a:=r{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}
.
Then we conclude using the hypothesis
t{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}a:=r{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}\in\llbracket\bot\rrbracket
.
∎

Lemma 66

(Adequacy for
{!}{}
)
.

If
\Delta;a:A\vdash t:\bot
such that

t\in\llbracket\bot\rrbracket
and for all
s\in\llbracket A\rrbracket
we get that
t{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}a:=s{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}\in\llbracket\bot\rrbracket
then this implies that
{!}{a.t}\in\llbracket{!}{A^{\perp}}\rrbracket
.

Proof.

Since
\llbracket{!}{A}\rrbracket=({?}\llbracket A^{\perp}\rrbracket)^{\perp}
then if
{?}u.r\in{?}\llbracket A^{\perp}\rrbracket
we must prove that

{!}{a.t}\lightning{?}u.r\in\llbracket\bot\rrbracket
.
Observe that the terms
t,r
are strongly normalizing by the proposition
54
. This means that the natural number
|t|+|r|
is well defined.

We prove this by induction on
|t|+|r|
. The base case is straightforward.
For the induction step we consider three different cases depending on the reduction step.

1.

If
t\to t
, this follows straight from induction.

2.

If
r\to r^{\prime}
, this follows straight from induction.

3.

If
{!}{a.t}\lightning{?}u.r\to r{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}u:=t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash\star{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}
then by the lemma
65
we get that

t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash\star{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}\in\llbracket A\rrbracket

and since by hypothesis
r\in{?}\llbracket A^{\perp}\rrbracket
then we can conclude that
r{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}u:=t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash\star{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}\in\llbracket\bot\rrbracket
.

∎

\Adequacy

*

Proof.

We prove this by induction on the derivation of the term
t
.
By the inductive hypothesis it suffices to consider substitutions such that their domain are the variables that became bound in the last step of the derivation.
Most of the proof follows from the respective lemmas proved earlier.

•

If
t=a
or
t=u
then by definition of the substitution
\sigma
we get that
t^{\sigma}\vartriangleleft_{A}\llbracket A\rrbracket
.

•

If
t=\langle t_{1},t_{2}\rangle
then the last step of the derivation is the following:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Delta;\Gamma\vdash t_{1}:A_{1}\quad\Delta;\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash t_{2}:A_{2}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=122.69427pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-i$\otimes$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 8.05547pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Delta;\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\langle t_{1},t_{2}\rangle:A_{1}\otimes A_{2}$}}}}\end{array}

We must prove that for all
\sigma\models\Delta,\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}
we have that
(\langle t_{1},t_{2}\rangle)^{\sigma}\vartriangleleft\llbracket A_{1}\otimes A_{2}\rrbracket
.

By hypothesis for all substitutions
\nu\models\Delta,\Gamma
and
\tau\models\Delta,\Gamma^{\prime}
we have that
t_{1}^{\nu}\in\llbracket A_{1}\rrbracket
and
t_{2}^{\tau}\in\llbracket A_{2}\rrbracket
.
In particular since
(\langle t_{1},t_{2}\rangle)^{\sigma}=\langle t_{1}^{\sigma},t_{2}^{\sigma}\rangle
thus without loss of generality it suffices to prove that
\langle t_{1},t_{2}\rangle\vartriangleleft\llbracket A_{1}\otimes A_{2}\rrbracket
.
Since
\llbracket A_{1}\otimes A_{2}\rrbracket=(\llbracket A_{1}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket A_{2}\rrbracket)^{\perp}{}^{\perp}
then using the lemma
53
we conclude this case.

•

If
t={\invamp}(a,b).t_{1}
then this follows from the lemma
59
.

•

If
t=t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\langle a,b\rangle:=s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}
then the last step of the derivation is the following:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Delta;\Gamma^{\prime},a:A_{1},b:A_{2}\vdash t_{1}:A\quad\Delta;\Gamma\vdash s:A_{1}\otimes A_{2}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=198.6673pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-e$\otimes$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 46.33313pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Delta;\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash{t_{1}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\langle a,b\rangle:=s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}:A$}}}}\end{array}

We must prove that for all
\sigma
such that
\sigma\models\Delta,\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}
we have that
(t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\langle a,b\rangle:=s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}})^{\sigma}\vartriangleleft\llbracket A\rrbracket
.

By hypothesis for all substitutions
\tau\models\Delta,\Gamma^{\prime},a:A_{1},b:A_{2}
and
\nu\models\Delta,\Gamma
we have that
t_{1}^{\tau}\in\llbracket A\rrbracket
and
s^{\nu}\in\llbracket A_{1}\otimes A_{2}\rrbracket
.
In particular we can consider compatible substitutions
\tau,\nu
such that
(t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\langle a,b\rangle:=s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}})^{\sigma}=t_{1}^{\tau}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\langle a,b\rangle:=s^{\nu}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}
, thus without loss of generality it suffices to prove that
t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\langle a,b\rangle:=s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\in\llbracket A\rrbracket
.

Using lemma
60
it suffices to prove that for all
r\in\llbracket A^{\perp}\rrbracket
the following holds
t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\langle a,b\rangle:=s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\lightning r\vartriangleleft_{\bot}\textrm{SN}_{\bot}
.
Using the structural equivalence we have that

t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\langle a,b\rangle:=s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\lightning r\equiv(t_{1}\lightning r){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\langle a,b\rangle:=s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\equiv({\invamp}(a,b).t_{1}\lightning r)\lightning s
,
thus we will prove that
({\invamp}(a,b).t_{1}\lightning r)\lightning s\vartriangleleft_{\bot}\textrm{SN}_{\bot}
.

Since
a,b\in\mathsf{fv}(t_{1})
and by inductive hypothesis for all compatible substitutions
\tau\models\Delta,\Gamma^{\prime},a:A_{1},b:A_{2}
we have that
t_{1}^{\tau}\in\llbracket A\rrbracket
then this implies that for all
s_{1}\in\llbracket A_{1}\rrbracket,s_{2}\in\llbracket A_{2}\rrbracket
we got that
t_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}a:=s_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}b:=s_{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}\in\llbracket A\rrbracket
.
Using the lemma
60
we have that since
t_{1}\in\llbracket A\rrbracket
and
r\in\llbracket A^{\perp}\rrbracket
then
t_{1}\lightning r\vartriangleleft_{\bot}\textrm{SN}_{\bot}
.
Then we can apply the lemma
59
to conclude that

{\invamp}(a,b).t_{1}\lightning r\in\llbracket A_{1}^{\perp}\invamp A_{2}^{\perp}\rrbracket
and this implies that
({\invamp}(a,b).t_{1}\lightning r)\lightning s\vartriangleleft_{\bot}\textrm{SN}_{\bot}
.

•

If
t=t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}t_{2}
then the last step of the derivation is the following:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Delta;\Gamma\vdash t_{1}:A_{1}\invamp A_{2}\quad\Delta;\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash t_{2}:A_{2}^{\perp}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=145.66646pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-e$\invamp_{2}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 31.64575pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Delta;\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}t_{2}:A_{1}$}}}}\end{array}

We must prove that for all
\sigma
such that
\sigma\models\Delta,\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}
we have that
(t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}t_{2})^{\sigma}\vartriangleleft\llbracket A\rrbracket
.

By hypothesis for all substitutions
\tau\models\Delta,\Gamma^{\prime}
and
\nu\models\Delta,\Gamma
we have that
t_{1}^{\tau}\in\llbracket A_{1}\invamp A_{2}\rrbracket
and
t_{2}^{\nu}\in\llbracket A_{2}^{\perp}\rrbracket
.
In particular we can consider compatible substitutions
\tau,\nu
such that
(t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}t_{2})^{\sigma}=t_{1}^{\tau}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}t_{2}^{\nu}
, thus without loss of generality it suffices to prove that
t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}t_{2}\in\llbracket A\rrbracket
.

Using lemma
60
it suffices to prove that for all
s\in\llbracket A_{1}^{\perp}\rrbracket
that
(t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}t_{2})\lightning s\vartriangleleft_{\bot}\textrm{SN}_{\bot}
.
Using the structural equivalence we have that
(t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}t_{2})\lightning s\equiv t_{1}\lightning(\langle s,t_{2}\rangle)
, thus it suffices to prove that
t_{1}\lightning(\langle s,t_{2}\rangle)\vartriangleleft_{\bot}\textrm{SN}_{\bot}
.
Since by hypothesis
t_{2}\in\llbracket A_{2}^{\perp}\rrbracket
and
s\in\llbracket A_{1}^{\perp}\rrbracket
then this implies that
\langle s,t_{2}\rangle\vartriangleleft\llbracket A_{1}^{\perp}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket A_{2}^{\perp}\rrbracket
and by the lemma
53
that
\langle s,t_{2}\rangle\vartriangleleft\llbracket A_{1}^{\perp}\otimes A_{2}^{\perp}\rrbracket
.
Since by hypothesis
t_{1}\in\llbracket A_{1}\invamp A_{2}\rrbracket
then we can conclude that
t_{1}\lightning(\langle s,t_{2}\rangle)\vartriangleleft_{\bot}\textrm{SN}_{\bot}
.

•

If
t=t_{1}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,t_{2}
then the last step of the derivation is the following:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Delta;\Gamma\vdash t_{1}:A_{1}\invamp A_{2}\quad\Delta;\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash t_{2}:A_{1}^{\perp}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=145.66646pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-e$\invamp_{1}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 29.97914pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Delta;\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash t_{1}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,t_{2}:A_{2}$}}}}\end{array}

We must prove that for all
\sigma
such that
\sigma\models\Delta,\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}
we have that
(t_{1}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,t_{2})^{\sigma}\vartriangleleft\llbracket A\rrbracket
.

By hypothesis for all substitutions
\tau\models\Delta,\Gamma^{\prime}
and
\nu\models\Delta,\Gamma
we have that
t_{1}^{\tau}\in\llbracket A_{1}\invamp A_{2}\rrbracket
and
t_{2}^{\nu}\in\llbracket A_{1}^{\perp}\rrbracket
.
In particular we can consider compatible substitutions
\tau,\nu
such that
(t_{1}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,t_{2})^{\sigma}=t_{1}^{\tau}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,t_{2}^{\nu}
, thus without loss of generality it suffices to prove that
t_{1}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,t_{2}\in\llbracket A\rrbracket
.

Using lemma
60
it suffices to prove that for all
s\in\llbracket A_{1}^{\perp}\rrbracket
that
(t_{1}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,t_{2})\lightning s\vartriangleleft_{\bot}\textrm{SN}_{\bot}

Using the structural equivalence we have that
(t_{1}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,t_{2})\lightning s\equiv(t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\blacktriangledown}s)\lightning t_{2}
, so that this case follows from the last one.

•

If
t={?}u.t_{1}
then the last step of the derivation is:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Delta,u:A^{\perp};\Gamma\vdash t_{1}:{\perp}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=80.82156pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-i${?}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 7.45824pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Delta;\Gamma\vdash{?}u.t_{1}:{?}A$}}}}\end{array}

We must prove that for all
\sigma
such that
\sigma\models\Delta,\Gamma
we have that
({?}u.t)^{\sigma}\vartriangleleft\llbracket{?}A\rrbracket
.

By hypothesis for all substitutions
\nu\models\Delta,\Gamma,u:A^{\perp}
we have that
t_{1}^{\nu}\vartriangleleft_{\bot}\textrm{SN}_{\bot}
, this implies that
({?}u.t_{1})^{\sigma}\in{?}\llbracket A^{\perp}\rrbracket
.
Using the lemma
53
we conclude that
({?}u.t_{1})^{\sigma}\in\llbracket{?}A\rrbracket
.

If
t={!}{a.t_{1}}
then this follows from the lemma
66
.

If
t=t_{1}\lightning t_{2}
then the last step of the derivation is the following:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Delta;\Gamma\vdash t_{1}:A\quad\Delta;\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash t_{2}:A^{\perp}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=119.47202pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-i${\perp}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 20.65273pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Delta;\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash t_{1}\lightning t_{2}:{\perp}$}}}}\end{array}

We must prove that for all
\sigma\models\Delta,\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}
we have that
(t_{1}\lightning t_{2})^{\sigma}\vartriangleleft_{\bot}\llbracket\bot\rrbracket=\textrm{SN}_{\bot}
.

By hypothesis for all substitutions
\nu\models\Delta,\Gamma
and
\tau\models\Delta,\Gamma^{\prime}
we have that
t_{1}^{\nu}\in\llbracket A\rrbracket
and
t_{2}^{\tau}\in\llbracket A^{\perp}\rrbracket
.
In particular we can consider compatible substitutions such that
(t_{1}\lightning t_{2})^{\sigma}=t_{1}^{\nu}\lightning t_{2}^{\tau}
thus using the lemma
60
we can conclude that
(t_{1}\lightning t_{2})^{\sigma}\vartriangleleft_{\bot}\textrm{SN}_{\bot}
.

If
t={t_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\blacktriangleright}^{a}t_{2}}
then the last step of the derivation is the following:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Delta;\Gamma\vdash t_{2}:{?}A\quad\Delta;a:A\vdash t_{1}:{\perp}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=127.73006pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-e${?}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 28.974pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Delta;\Gamma\vdash{t_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\blacktriangleright}^{a}t_{2}}:{\perp}$}}}}\end{array}

We must prove that for all
\sigma\models\Delta,\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}
we have that
({t_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\blacktriangleright}^{a}t_{2}})^{\sigma}\in\textrm{SN}_{\bot}
.

By hypothesis for all substitutions
\nu\models\Delta,\Gamma
and
\tau\models\Delta,a:A
we have that
t_{2}^{\nu}\vartriangleleft\llbracket{?}A\rrbracket
and
t_{1}^{\tau}\vartriangleleft\llbracket\bot\rrbracket
. In particular we can consider compatible substitutions such that
({t_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\blacktriangleright}^{a}t_{2}})^{\sigma}={t_{1}^{\tau}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\blacktriangleright}^{a}t_{2}^{\nu}}
thus without loss of generality it suffices to prove that
{t_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\blacktriangleright}^{a}t_{2}}\in\llbracket{?}A\rrbracket
.

Using lemma
60
it suffices to prove that for all
r\in\llbracket\text{1}\rrbracket
that
{t_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\blacktriangleright}^{a}t_{2}}\lightning r\vartriangleleft_{\bot}\textrm{SN}_{\bot}
.
By the structural equivalence we get
{t_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\blacktriangleright}^{a}t_{2}}\lightning r\equiv({!}{a.t_{1}}\lightning s){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}
and since
r\in\llbracket\text{1}\rrbracket=\textrm{SN}_{\text{1}}
then
r
is strongly normalizing,
thus it suffices to prove that
{!}{a.t_{1}}\lightning s\vartriangleleft_{\bot}\textrm{SN}_{\bot}
and this follows from the lemma
66
.

•

If
t=t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}{!}{u}:=t_{2}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}
then the last step of the derivation is the following:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Delta;\Gamma\vdash t_{2}:{!}{A}\quad\Delta,u:A;\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash t_{1}:C$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=140.08678pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-e${!}{}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 20.4166pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Delta;\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}{!}{u}:=t_{2}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}:C$}}}}\end{array}

We must prove that for all
\sigma\models\Delta,\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}
we have that
(t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}{!}{u}:=t_{2}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}})^{\sigma}\in\llbracket C\rrbracket
.

By hypothesis for all substitutions
\nu\models\Delta,\Gamma
and
\tau\models\Delta,u:A,\Gamma^{\prime}
we have that
t_{2}^{\nu}\in\llbracket{!}{A}\rrbracket
and
t_{1}^{\tau}\in\llbracket C\rrbracket
.
In particular we can consider compatible substitutions such that
(t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}{!}{u}:=t_{2}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}})^{\sigma}=t_{1}^{\tau}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}{!}{u}:=t_{2}^{\nu}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}
thus it suffices to prove that
t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}{!}{u}:=t_{2}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\in\llbracket C\rrbracket
.

Using lemma
60
it suffices to prove that for all
r\in\llbracket C^{\perp}\rrbracket
that
t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}{!}{u}:=t_{2}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\lightning r\vartriangleleft_{\bot}\textrm{SN}_{\bot}
.
Using the structural equivalence we get that
t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}{!}{u}:=t_{2}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\lightning r\equiv{?}u.(t_{1}\lightning r)\lightning t_{2}
thus it suffices to prove that
{?}u.(t_{1}\lightning r)\lightning t_{2}\vartriangleleft_{\bot}\textrm{SN}_{\bot}
.

Since
r\in\llbracket C^{\perp}\rrbracket
then we get that
t_{1}\lightning r\vartriangleleft_{\bot}\textrm{SN}_{\bot}
and by hypothesis for all
s\in\llbracket A\rrbracket
we have that
(t_{1}\lightning r){\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}a:=s{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}\vartriangleleft_{\bot}\textrm{SN}_{\bot}
.
This implies that
{?}u.(t_{1}\lightning r)\in{?}\llbracket A^{\perp}\rrbracket
and by the lemma
53
then
{?}u.(t_{1}\lightning r)\in\llbracket{?}A^{\perp}\rrbracket
.
Since
t_{2}\in\llbracket{!}{A}\rrbracket
then we conclude that
{?}u.(t_{1}\lightning r)\lightning t_{2}\in\textrm{SN}_{\bot}
.

If
t=t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=t_{2}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}
then the last step of the derivation is the following:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Delta;\Gamma\vdash t_{2}:\text{1}\quad\Delta;\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash t_{1}:A$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=108.4443pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-e$\text{1}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 6.52777pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Delta;\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=t_{2}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}:A$}}}}\end{array}

We must prove that for all
\sigma\models\Delta,\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}
we have that
(t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=t_{2}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}})^{\sigma}\in\llbracket A\rrbracket
.

\nu\models\Delta,\Gamma
and
\tau\models\Delta,\Gamma^{\prime}
we have that
t_{2}^{\nu}\in\llbracket\text{1}\rrbracket
and
t_{1}^{\tau}\in\llbracket A\rrbracket
.
In particular we can consider compatible substitutions such that
(t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}{!}{u}:=t_{2}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}})^{\sigma}=t_{1}^{\tau}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=t_{2}^{\nu}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}
thus it suffices to prove that
t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=t_{2}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\in\llbracket A\rrbracket
.

Using lemma
60
it suffices to prove that for all
r\in\llbracket A^{\perp}\rrbracket
that
t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=t_{2}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\lightning r\vartriangleleft_{\bot}\textrm{SN}_{\bot}
.
Using the structural equivalence we get that
t_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=t_{2}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\lightning r\equiv(t_{1}\lightning r){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=t_{2}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}
, thus it suffices to prove that
(t_{1}\lightning r){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=t_{2}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\vartriangleleft_{\bot}\textrm{SN}_{\bot}
.
Since by the inductive hypothesis
t_{1}\lightning r\vartriangleleft_{\bot}\textrm{SN}_{\bot}
and since
t_{2}\in\llbracket\text{1}\rrbracket=\textrm{SN}_{\text{1}}
then we conclude that
(t_{1}\lightning r){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=t_{2}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\vartriangleleft_{\bot}\textrm{SN}_{\bot}
.

∎

Appendix E
Weak Church Rosser

Lemma 67
.

Let
\mathsf{L}
be a positive eliminator context. Then the following items hold:

1.

({\invamp}(a,b).t)\mathsf{L}
is well-typed iff
{\invamp}(a,b).t\mathsf{L}
is well-typed.

2.

({?}u.t)\mathsf{L}
is well-typed iff
{?}u.t\mathsf{L}
is well-typed.

3.

If
\mathsf{dom}(\mathsf{L})\cap\mathsf{fv}(s)=\varnothing
, then
(t\lightning s)\mathsf{L}
is well-typed iff
t\mathsf{L}\lightning s
is well-typed. Similarly,
(s\lightning t)\mathsf{L}
is well-typed iff
s\lightning t\mathsf{L}
is well-typed.

Proof.

This follows from the fact that in terms of the form
t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\langle a,b\rangle:=s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}
,
t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}{!}{u}:=s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}
and
t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\star:=s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}
, the typing rules
m-e
\otimes
,
m-e
{!}{}
, and
m-e
1
, resp., place no requirement on the type of
t
and allow any number of linear and unrestricted variables.
∎

Lemma 68
.

Let
\mathsf{L}
be a positive eliminator context. Then the following items hold:

1.

({\invamp}(a,b).t)\mathsf{L}\equiv{\invamp}(a,b).t\mathsf{L}
.

2.

({?}u.t)\mathsf{L}\equiv{?}u.t\mathsf{L}
.

3.

(t\lightning s)\mathsf{L}\equiv t\mathsf{L}\lightning s
and
(s\lightning t)\mathsf{L}\equiv s\lightning t\mathsf{L}
, if
\mathsf{dom}(\mathsf{L})\cap\mathsf{fv}(s)=\varnothing
.

4.

t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\langle a,b\rangle:=s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\mathsf{L}\equiv t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\langle a,b\rangle:=s\mathsf{L}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}
, if
\mathsf{dom}(\mathsf{L})\cap\mathsf{fv}(s)\varnothing
.

5.

t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}{!}{u}:=s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\mathsf{L}\equiv t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}{!}{u}:=s\mathsf{L}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}
, if
\mathsf{dom}(\mathsf{L})\cap\mathsf{fv}(s)=\varnothing
.

6.

t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\mathtt{p}:=s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\mathsf{L}\equiv t\mathsf{L}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}\mathtt{p}:=s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}
, if
\mathsf{fv}(\mathsf{L})\cap\mathsf{fv}(\mathtt{p})=\varnothing
.

Proof.

All the items can be proved by induction on
\mathsf{L}
.

∎

Lemma 69

(Reduction is compatible with substitution of linear variables)
.

Suppose
t\to_{\mathsf{\lambda_{\textsf{{MELL}}}}}^{-}s
. Then

1.

t{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}a:=r{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}\to_{\mathsf{\lambda_{\textsf{{MELL}}}}}^{-}s{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}a:=r{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}
.

2.

r{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}a:=t{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}\to_{\mathsf{\lambda_{\textsf{{MELL}}}}}^{-}r{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}a:=s{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}
.

Proof.

By induction on
t
.
∎

Lemma 70

(Reduction is compatible with substitution of unrestricted variables)
.

Suppose
t\to_{\mathsf{\lambda_{\textsf{{MELL}}}}}^{-}s
. Then

1.

t{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}u:=r{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}\to_{\mathsf{\lambda_{\textsf{{MELL}}}}}^{-}s{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}u:=r{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}
.

2.

r{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}u:=t{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}\twoheadrightarrow_{\mathsf{\lambda_{\textsf{{MELL}}}}}^{-}r{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}u:=s{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}
.

Proof.

By induction on
t
.
∎

Lemma 71
.

Let
t
and
t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}
be typable terms.

1.

t\to_{\mathsf{\lambda_{\textsf{{MELL}}}}}^{-}s
implies
t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}\to_{\mathsf{\lambda_{\textsf{{MELL}}}}}s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}
.

2.

r\to_{\mathsf{\lambda_{\textsf{{MELL}}}}}^{-}s
implies
t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash r{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}\to_{\mathsf{\lambda_{\textsf{{MELL}}}}}t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}
.

Proof.

Both items are proved simultaneously by induction on
t
.

∎

Lemma 72

(name=Weak Church-Rosser)
.

Let
t
be typed. Then
t\to_{\mathsf{\lambda_{\textsf{{MELL}}}}}{}s
and
t\to_{\mathsf{\lambda_{\textsf{{MELL}}}}}{}r
, implies there exists
p
such that
s\twoheadrightarrow_{\mathsf{\lambda_{\textsf{{MELL}}}}}p
and
r\twoheadrightarrow_{\mathsf{\lambda_{\textsf{{MELL}}}}}p
.

Proof.

Since
\equiv
is a strong-bisimulation, it suffices to consider the case where
t\to_{\mathsf{\lambda_{\textsf{{MELL}}}}}^{-}{}s
through a step
\sigma
and
t\to_{\mathsf{\lambda_{\textsf{{MELL}}}}}^{-}{}r
through a step
\rho
. By induction on
t
.

∎

Appendix F
Translations

F.1
Parigot’s
\lambda\mu

F.1.1
T-Translation

\TypePreservationForTTranslation

*

Proof.

By induction on the derivation.

1.

p-ax
. The derivation ends in

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 39.67337pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=79.34674pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{p-ax}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma,x:A\vdash x:A\mid\Sigma$}}}}\end{array}

Note that
{x}^{\mathsf{T}}_{k}=x\lightning{!}{k}
and thus we conclude from:

Lemma 73
.

Suppose
M\to_{\beta}N
, then
{M}^{\mathsf{T}}_{k}\to_{\mathsf{\lambda_{\textsf{{MELL}}}}}{N}^{\mathsf{T}}_{k}
.

Proof.

By induction on
M
.

∎

Lemma 74
.

Suppose
M\to_{\mu}N
, then
{M}^{\mathsf{T}}_{k}={N}^{\mathsf{T}}_{k}
.

Proof.

By induction on
M
.

∎

Lemma 75
.

Suppose
M\to_{\rho}N
, then
{M}^{\mathsf{T}}_{k}\equiv{N}^{\mathsf{T}}_{k}
.

Proof.

By induction on
M
.

∎

Lemma 76
.

Suppose
M\to_{\theta}N
, then
{M}^{\mathsf{T}}_{k}\equiv{N}^{\mathsf{T}}_{k}
.

Proof.

By induction on
M
.

∎

\CalcMELLSimulatesCalcParigotViaTTranslation

{\Gamma}^{\mathsf{\underline{Q}}},({\Sigma}^{\mathsf{Q}})^{\perp},a:({A}^{\mathsf{Q}})^{\perp},k^{\prime}:({A}^{\mathsf{Q}})^{\perp};\cdot\vdash{N}^{\mathsf{Q}}_{k^{\prime}}:{\perp}\end{array}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=181.04277pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-sub}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 9.03064pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\Gamma}^{\mathsf{\underline{Q}}},({\Sigma}^{\mathsf{Q}})^{\perp},a:({A}^{\mathsf{Q}})^{\perp};\cdot\vdash{N}^{\mathsf{Q}}_{k^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}k^{\prime}:=a{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}:{\perp}$}}}}\end{array}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=222.76505pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-i${\perp}$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 10.34328pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\Gamma}^{\mathsf{\underline{Q}}},({\Sigma}^{\mathsf{Q}})^{\perp},a:({A}^{\mathsf{Q}})^{\perp},k:\text{1};\cdot\vdash{N}^{\mathsf{Q}}_{k^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}k^{\prime}:=a{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}\lightning k:{\perp}\par$}}}}\end{array}

5.

p-mu
. The derivation ends in

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma\vdash N:{\perp}\mid\Sigma,a:A$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=77.32716pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{p-mu}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 2.40392pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash\mu{a}.N:A\mid\Sigma$}}}}\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\begin{array}[]{c}\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 1.6667pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\begin{array}[]{c}\text{IH }\\
{\Gamma}^{\mathsf{\underline{Q}}},({\Sigma}^{\mathsf{Q}})^{\perp},a:({A}^{\mathsf{Q}})^{\perp},k^{\prime}:\text{1};\cdot\vdash{N}^{\mathsf{Q}}_{k^{\prime}}:{\perp}\end{array}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=159.91788pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-sub}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\Gamma}^{\mathsf{\underline{Q}}},({\Sigma}^{\mathsf{Q}})^{\perp},a:({A}^{\mathsf{Q}})^{\perp};\cdot\vdash{N}^{\mathsf{Q}}_{k^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}k^{\prime}:=\star{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}:{\perp}$}}}}\end{array}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=201.64015pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-sub}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 2.28476pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\Gamma}^{\mathsf{\underline{Q}}},({\Sigma}^{\mathsf{Q}})^{\perp},k:({A}^{\mathsf{Q}})^{\perp};\cdot\vdash{N}^{\mathsf{Q}}_{k^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}k^{\prime}:=\star{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}a:=k{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}:{\perp}$}}}}\end{array}

∎

.

.

.

F.3
Hasegawa’s
\mu\textsf{DCLL}

Hasegawa
[
19
,
20
]
introduces
\mu\textsf{DCLL}
, an extension of the Dual Intuitionistic Linear Logic of Barber and Plotkin
[
9
,
8
]
to
MELL
. The system features both the intuitionistic (non-linear) arrow type
\supset
and the linear arrow type
\multimap
.

Definition 82

(Types and terms of
\mu\textsf{DCLL}
)
.

They are defined as follows:

\begin{array}[]{r@{\hspace{.1cm}}c@{\hspace{.1cm}}l@{\hspace{.5cm}}r@{\hspace{.1cm}}c@{\hspace{.1cm}}l}A,B\hskip 2.84544pt&::=\hfil\hskip 2.84544pt&{\perp}\,|\,\alpha\,|\,A\supset B\,|\,A\multimap B\hfil\hskip 14.22636pt&M,N\hskip 2.84544pt&::=\hfil\hskip 2.84544pt&x\,|\,u\,|\,\Lambda u.\,M\,|\,M\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{@}}\,N\,|\,\lambda x.\,M\,|\,M\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,N\,|\,[a]M\,|\,\mu{a}.M\end{array}

Typing rules for
\mu\textsf{DCLL}
are given below, where in
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}
one assumes that
\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma)\cap\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime})=\varnothing
and similarly for
\Sigma,\Sigma^{\prime}
.

\begin{array}[]{c}\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 38.77997pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=77.55994pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{int-ax}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Delta,u:A;\cdot\vdash u:A\mid\cdot$}}}}\end{array}\quad\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 37.38173pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=74.76346pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{lin-ax}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Delta;x:A\vdash x:A\mid\cdot$}}}}\end{array}\par\\
\\
\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 5.97223pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Delta,u:A;\Gamma\vdash M:B\mid\Sigma$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=109.74146pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{$\supset$-I}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Delta;\Gamma\vdash\Lambda u.\,M:A\supset B\mid\Sigma$}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Delta;\Gamma\vdash M:A\supset B\mid\Sigma\quad\Delta;\cdot\vdash N:A\mid\cdot$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=157.58617pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{$\supset$-E}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 32.77768pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Delta;\Gamma\vdash M\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{@}}\,N:B\mid\Sigma$}}}}\end{array}\\
\\
\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 7.08336pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Delta;\Gamma,x:A\vdash M:B\mid\Sigma$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=111.9544pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{$\multimap$-I}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Delta;\Gamma\vdash\lambda x.\,M:A\multimap B\mid\Sigma$}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Delta;\Gamma\vdash M:A\multimap B\mid\Sigma\quad\Delta;\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash N:A\mid\Sigma^{\prime}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=178.33609pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{$\multimap$-E}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 30.83322pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Delta;\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash M\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,N:B\mid\Sigma,\Sigma^{\prime}$}}}}\end{array}\\
\\
\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 15.70255pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Delta;\Gamma\vdash M:A\mid\Sigma$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=102.61308pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{${\perp}$-I}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Delta;\Gamma\vdash[a]M:{\perp}\mid\Sigma,a:A$}}}}\end{array}\quad\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Delta;\Gamma\vdash M:{\perp}\mid\Sigma,a:A$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=91.77162pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{${\perp}$-E}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 2.40392pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Delta;\Gamma\vdash\mu{a}.M:A\mid\Sigma$}}}}\end{array}\end{array}

Our presentation of equality in
\mu\textsf{DCLL}
differs slightly from that of
[
19
,
20
]
in three ways. First, we distinguish linear variables (
e.g.

x
) from non-linear variables (
e.g.

u
). Second, the two extensionality axioms have been removed (
i.e.

\Lambda x.\,M\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{@}}\,x\doteq M
and
\lambda x.\,M\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,x\doteq M
, with
x\notin\mathsf{fv}(M)
). In addition,
op cit
uses a “one-shot”
\mu
-binder in their presentation of (
\mu-R
). That is, in the right-hand side of (
\mu-R
),
op cit
removes both the occurrence of the
\mu
-binder and the (unique) occurrence of
[a]
in
M
, whereas we retain both
5
5

5

More precisely, the equation is
N\,\,(\mu{a}.M)\doteq M[^{N(-)}/[a](-)]
, where
M[^{N(-)}/[a](-)]
is obtained by replacing the unique occurrence of
[a]O
by
N\,\,O
in a capture-free way.
. Our approach is also the one taken in the classical linear
\lambda\mu
-calculus of Bierman
[
11
]
.

Definition 83

(Axioms for
\mu\textsf{DCLL}
)
.

\mu\textsf{DCLL}
-equivalence is defined as the contextual closure of the following equivalence axioms:

\begin{array}[]{llll}(\Lambda u.\,M)\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{@}}\,N&\doteq_{lin\beta}&M{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}u:=N{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}\\
(\lambda x.\,M)\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,N&\doteq_{\beta}&M{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\{}}x:=N{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bm{\}}}\\
\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{llll}N\,\,(\mu{a}.M)&\doteq_{\mu-R}&\mu{a}.(M{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\bm{\{}}a{\lhd^{\star}}N{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\bm{\}}})\\
\mu{a}.[a]M&\doteq_{\theta}&M&(a\notin\mathsf{fv}(M))\end{array}

Definition 84

(Translation)
.

The translation of a formula and
\mu\textsf{DCLL}
-term are given by

\begin{array}[]{llll}{{\perp}}^{\mathsf{H}}&:=&{\perp}\\
{\alpha}^{\mathsf{H}}&:=&\alpha\\
{(A\supset B)}^{\mathsf{H}}&:=&{?}({A}^{\mathsf{H}})^{\perp}\invamp{B}^{\mathsf{H}}\\
{(A\multimap B)}^{\mathsf{H}}&:=&({A}^{\mathsf{H}})^{\perp}\invamp{B}^{\mathsf{H}}\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{llll}{x}^{\mathsf{H}}&:=&x\\
{(\Lambda u.\,M)}^{\mathsf{H}}&:=&{\invamp}(a,k).({M}^{\mathsf{H}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}{!}{u}:=a{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\lightning k)\\
{(M\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{@}}\,N)}^{\mathsf{H}}&:=&{M}^{\mathsf{H}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,{!}{{N}^{\mathsf{H}}}\\
{(\lambda x.\,M)}^{\mathsf{H}}&:=&{\invamp}(x,k).({M}^{\mathsf{H}}\lightning k)\\
{(M\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,N)}^{\mathsf{H}}&:=&{M}^{\mathsf{H}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bullet}\,{N}^{\mathsf{H}}\\
{([a]M)}^{\mathsf{H}}&:=&{M}^{\mathsf{H}}\lightning a\\
{(\mu{a}.M)}^{\mathsf{H}}&:=&{M}^{\mathsf{H}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\{}}a\backslash\!\!\backslash\star{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{\}}}\par\end{array}

Lemma 85
.

If
\Delta;\Gamma\vdash M:A\mid\Sigma
holds in
\mu\textsf{DCLL}
, then
{\Delta}^{\mathsf{H}};{\Gamma}^{\mathsf{H}},({\Sigma}^{\mathsf{H}})^{\perp}\vdash{M}^{\mathsf{H}}:{A}^{\mathsf{H}}
holds in
\lambda_{\textsf{{MELL}}}
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of
\Delta;\Gamma\vdash M:A\mid\Sigma
.

1.

int-ax

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 38.77997pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=77.55994pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{int-ax}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Delta,u:A;\cdot\vdash u:A\mid\cdot$}}}}\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 37.25925pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\vphantom{{}^{@}}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=81.15741pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-uax}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\Delta}^{\mathsf{H}},u:{A}^{\mathsf{H}};\cdot\vdash u:{A}^{\mathsf{H}}$}}}}\end{array}

lin-ax

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 37.38173pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=74.76346pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{lin-ax}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Delta;x:A\vdash x:A\mid\cdot$}}}}\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 34.47215pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\vphantom{{}^{@}}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=75.58322pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{m-ax}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\Delta}^{\mathsf{H}};x:{A}^{\mathsf{H}}\vdash x:{A}^{\mathsf{H}}$}}}}\end{array}

\supset
-I

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 5.97223pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Delta,u:A;\Gamma\vdash M:B\mid\Sigma$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=109.74146pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{{$\supset$-I}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Delta;\Gamma\vdash\Lambda u.\,M:A\supset B\mid\Sigma$}}}}\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\begin{array}[]{c}\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 21.45126pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\begin{array}[]{c}\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 17.92476pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty{\Delta}^{\mathsf{H}},u:{A}^{\mathsf{H}};{\Gamma}^{\mathsf{H}},({\Sigma}^{\mathsf{H}})^{\perp}\vdash{M}^{\mathsf{H}}:{B}^{\mathsf{H}}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=162.14714pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\Delta}^{\mathsf{H}};{\Gamma}^{\mathsf{H}},a:{!}{{A}^{\mathsf{H}}},({\Sigma}^{\mathsf{H}})^{\perp}\vdash{M}^{\mathsf{H}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}{!}{u}:=a{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}:{B}^{\mathsf{H}}$}}}}\end{array}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=220.04967pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\Delta}^{\mathsf{H}};{\Gamma}^{\mathsf{H}},a:{!}{{A}^{\mathsf{H}}},({\Sigma}^{\mathsf{H}})^{\perp},k:({B}^{\mathsf{H}})^{\perp}\vdash{M}^{\mathsf{H}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}{!}{u}:=a{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\lightning k:{\perp}$}}}}\end{array}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=235.04967pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsc{}$}}}\hbox{\kern 6.66655pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\Delta}^{\mathsf{H}};{\Gamma}^{\mathsf{H}},({\Sigma}^{\mathsf{H}})^{\perp}\vdash{\invamp}(a,k).{M}^{\mathsf{H}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{[}}{!}{u}:=a{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{]}}\lightning k:{?}({A}^{\mathsf{H}})^{\perp}\invamp{B}^{\mathsf{H}}$}}}}\end{array}
